# NopeCore API

Generated by `scripts/check-docs.sh` from the DocC comments in `Sources/NopeCore`. Do not edit by hand.

## `ApertureRange`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ApertureRange.swift`

```swift
struct ApertureRange
```

The f-numbers a lens supports.

Most iPhone lenses have a fixed aperture, where ``minimumFNumber`` equals ``maximumFNumber``
and ``recommendedStops`` has one value. Lenses with a variable aperture (iOS 27 and later)
report a range (capability survey, item 5).

## `ApertureRange/fixed(_:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ApertureRange.swift`

```swift
static func fixed(_ fNumber: Double) -> ApertureRange
```

A fixed aperture with one f-number.

- Parameter fNumber: The lens's f-number.
- Returns: A range whose minimum, maximum and only stop are `fNumber`.


## `AstroModel`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AstroHelper.swift`

```swift
@MainActor final class AstroModel
```

The astro helper: a consistent series of single Bayer RAW frames of the night sky, to be
stacked in other software (issue #60).

The app never stacks, merges or processes the frames: each one is an ordinary single-frame
photo that goes through the metadata allowlist, read-back and signing like any other
(AGENTS.md, "Honesty"). The helper only sets up the camera and takes the frames:

- **Turning it on** turns manual controls on if they are off, sets focus to the far end of
  the lens's range, the shutter to the longest the lens allows and ISO to the highest
  (``startingExposure(for:)``), turns the light off, returns the drive mode to single and
  turns bracketing off. After that, every manual control is the person's to change; the
  helper doesn't change them again until it is turned on again or the lens changes.
- **The shutter** then starts the self-timer (``startDelay``), so touching the phone doesn't
  shake the first frame, and takes ``frameCount`` frames back to back, each saved while the
  next is exposed.
- **A run pauses** (``isPaused``, issue #64) when the app leaves the foreground or the camera
  pauses (a call, another app, heat): the frames taken are saved, and a frame cut short is
  taken again. When the app is open and the camera runs again, the start delay counts down
  once more and the run continues, after storage and battery are checked again. Leaving during
  the start delay starts the run paused.
- **A run stops** when the person stops it (also while paused), when the next frame wouldn't
  fit in storage, when the battery is at or below ``stopBatteryLevel`` and not charging, when
  the camera stops for good (it failed), or at the first frame that can't be taken or saved
  in the foreground. Every frame taken before that is saved.
- **Turning it off** turns manual controls off again if the helper turned them on, and night
  mode off again if the helper turned it on and the person hasn't changed it since (unless
  ``NopeSettings/keepsNightModeAfterAstro`` is on).

``CameraModel`` owns one as ``CameraModel/astro``; an interface reads it and sends
``AstroIntent`` values. An interface's Astro control turns it on and off with
``CameraModel/setAstro(_:settings:)``, which also turns night mode on, because night mode is a
setting of the screens, kept in ``NopeSettings``, not of the camera. The helper is off at every
launch (AGENTS.md, "Cognitive": no surprise modes), so the camera screen calls
``CameraModel/forgetNightModeFromAstroAtLaunch(settings:)`` once: after a relaunch night
mode stays as it was and is the person's.

## `AstroModel/startingExposure(for:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AstroHelper.swift`

```swift
@MainActor static func startingExposure(for lens: Lens) -> AstroExposure?
```

The exposure the helper starts from on a lens: the longest shutter and the highest ISO
the lens reports, and focus at the far end of its range (lens position 1, the farthest
the lens focuses; AVFoundation, `AVCaptureDevice.lensPosition`). The lens position isn't
calibrated to a distance, so the far end may not be exactly sharp at infinity: the person
checks it with the focus magnifier and fine-tunes it.

- Parameter lens: A lens.
- Returns: The values, or `nil` when the lens lacks Bayer RAW, manual exposure, a
  shutter or ISO range, or manual focus.

## `AstroModel/stopBatteryLevel`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AstroHelper.swift`

```swift
@MainActor static let stopBatteryLevel: Double
```

At or below this charge, unplugged, a run stops before the next frame: 10%. An
assumption to be checked on a device: low enough not to stop a run the person can still
finish, high enough to leave charge for the frames to save and the phone to be used.

## `AutoControlIntent`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AutoControls.swift`

```swift
enum AutoControlIntent
```

A request that works in full auto as well as with manual controls, wrapped in
``CameraIntent/auto(_:)``: tap to focus and expose, the focus and exposure lock, exposure
compensation, the Close-Up and Far Focus limits, face detection, the fast shutter rule, the
close-up suggestion and the focus magnifier.

None of them changes a manual setting: while a control is set manually, the camera keeps the
person's value, and a tap only aims what is still automatic (issue #56, "Manual controls: up
to the person").

## `AutoShutter`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AutoControls.swift`

```swift
enum AutoShutter
```

The fast shutter rule of full auto, and when to say "Hold it steady or use a tripod"
(issue #56).

Full auto lets the camera's auto exposure choose, but no slower than the shortest shutter
that still exposes well, from the shortest of: the ``FastShutter`` choice, and the handheld
rule. When even the handheld limit can't expose the scene at the lens's highest ISO, the
limit is lifted, so the photo is still exposed well, and the person is told to hold the
camera steady.

The brightness the scene needs is the product of ISO and exposure time corrected by the
meter's offset: `ISO × time × 2^(−offset)` (`AVCaptureDevice.exposureTargetOffset` is in EV,
negative when the picture is darker than the target). The product doesn't depend on how the
camera splits it between ISO and time, so it can be compared with what a limit allows:
`highest ISO × limit`. The aperture is fixed on every lens the app uses in full auto.

## `AutoShutter/handheldLimit(for:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AutoControls.swift`

```swift
static func handheldLimit(for lens: Lens) -> Double?
```

The handheld rule: 1/(2 × 35 mm-equivalent focal length) seconds, inside the lens's
shutter range.

- Parameter lens: The lens.
- Returns: The slowest handheld-safe shutter in seconds, or `nil` when the lens reports
  no focal length or no shutter range.

## `AutoShutter/limits(for:fastShutter:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AutoControls.swift`

```swift
static func limits(for lens: Lens, fastShutter: FastShutter) -> [Double]
```

The limits full auto tries, shortest first: the fast shutter choice, then the handheld
rule. Each is inside the lens's shutter range.

- Parameters:
  - lens: The lens.
  - fastShutter: The person's choice.
- Returns: The limits; empty when the lens reports no shutter range.

## `AutoShutter/longestShutter(for:lens:fastShutter:current:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AutoControls.swift`

```swift
static func longestShutter(for reading: ExposureReading, lens: Lens, fastShutter: FastShutter, current: Double?) -> Double?
```

The longest shutter full auto allows for a reading.

- Parameters:
  - reading: What the camera is using now.
  - lens: The lens.
  - fastShutter: The person's choice.
  - current: The limit in use now, or `nil` when it is lifted.
- Returns: The first limit the scene is bright enough for, or `nil` to lift the limit

## `AutoShutter/predatesLimit(_:limit:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AutoControls.swift`

```swift
static func predatesLimit(_ reading: ExposureReading, limit: Double?) -> Bool
```

Whether a full-auto reading was taken before the camera applied the limit in use, so it
says nothing about the scene under that limit.

- Parameters:
  - reading: The reading.
  - limit: The limit in use, in seconds, or `nil` when it is lifted.
- Returns: `true` when the reading's shutter is longer than the limit by more than
  ``predatesLimitMargin``; `false` when the limit is lifted.

## `AutoShutter/predatesLimitMargin`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AutoControls.swift`

```swift
static let predatesLimitMargin: Double
```

How much longer than full auto's limit a reading's shutter may be and still count as
taken under that limit: a quarter stop. Auto exposure keeps the shutter within
`AVCaptureDevice.activeMaxExposureDuration`, so a reading clearly longer than the limit
was taken before the camera applied it (after a manual or astro exposure, or on the
previous lens). The margin allows for a reported shutter rounded to the sensor's timing;
its size is an assumption, checked on the device (docs/TESTING.md).

## `AutoShutter/returnMargin`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AutoControls.swift`

```swift
static let returnMargin: Double
```

How much brighter than needed a limit must be before a lifted limit comes back: half a
stop, so the limit doesn't switch on and off when the light is near the threshold. A
tool setting, chosen to be larger than the meter's frame-to-frame changes (an
assumption, checked on the device: docs/TESTING.md).

## `AutoShutter/risksBlur(_:on:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AutoControls.swift`

```swift
static func risksBlur(_ duration: Double, on lens: Lens) -> Bool
```

Whether a shutter is slow enough to risk blur from hand shake, for the hint in manual
controls.

- Parameters:
  - duration: The shutter in use or chosen, in seconds.
  - lens: The lens.
- Returns: `true` when it is longer than ``handheldLimit(for:)``; `false` without a limit.

## `BackgroundTimeRequesting`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/LiveActivity.swift`

```swift
@MainActor protocol BackgroundTimeRequesting : AnyObject
```

Asks iOS for a little time to finish saving photos after the app leaves the foreground. The app
implements it with `UIApplication.beginBackgroundTask(withName:expirationHandler:)`, which is
a limited extension of the app's run time, not a background mode (AGENTS.md, "Battery"); tests
pass a fake.

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Bracketing.swift`


The ISO.

## `BracketFrame/plan(offsets:base:shutterFirst:isoRange:durationRange:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Bracketing.swift`

```swift
static func plan(offsets: [Double], base: BracketFrame, shutterFirst: Bool, isoRange: ClosedRange<Double>, durationRange: ClosedRange<Double>) -> [BracketFrame]
```

Plans the frames of a bracket around a base exposure, within the lens's ranges.

Each frame changes the exposure by its offset: first by changing the parameter the person
didn't set (the shutter when ISO is set, otherwise ISO when the shutter is set, otherwise
the shutter), then by the other parameter for whatever the first can't reach. A frame
beyond both limits gets the nearest exposure the lens can give.

- Parameters:
  - offsets: Each frame's offset in EV (``BracketSettings/offsets``).
  - base: The base ISO and exposure duration.
  - shutterFirst: Whether to change the shutter before ISO.
  - isoRange: The lens's ISO range.
  - durationRange: The lens's exposure duration range, in seconds.
- Returns: One frame per offset, in the same order.

## `BracketFramePlan`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Bracketing.swift`

```swift
struct BracketFramePlan
```

One frame of a planned bracket as an interface lists it (docs/design/BRACKETING.md,
section 4, "The Frames"): its number, the offset asked for, the offset the lens can really
give, and the exposure the camera is asked for.

A frame beyond the lens's ISO and shutter ranges gets the nearest exposure the lens can give
(``BracketFrame/plan(offsets:base:shutterFirst:isoRange:durationRange:)``); ``isLimited`` marks
it so an interface can say so before the photo is taken.

## `BracketFramePlan/plan(offsets:base:shutterFirst:isoRange:durationRange:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Bracketing.swift`

```swift
static func plan(offsets: [Double], base: BracketFrame, shutterFirst: Bool, isoRange: ClosedRange<Double>, durationRange: ClosedRange<Double>) -> [BracketFramePlan]
```

Plans every frame of a bracket around a base exposure, within the lens's ranges, and
marks the frames the lens can't give.

- Parameters:
  - offsets: Each frame's offset in EV, in the order taken (``BracketSettings/offsets``).
  - base: The base ISO and exposure duration.
  - shutterFirst: Whether to change the shutter before ISO.
  - isoRange: The lens's ISO range.
  - durationRange: The lens's exposure duration range, in seconds.
- Returns: One plan per offset, in the same order.

## `BracketFramePlan/requestedOffset`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Bracketing.swift`

```swift
let requestedOffset: Double
```

The offset asked for, in EV, relative to the base exposure.

## `BracketFramePlan/tolerance`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Bracketing.swift`

```swift
static let tolerance: Double
```

How far, in EV, the offset reached may differ from the one asked for and still count as
reached: the planning multiplies and clamps floating-point values, so a reached frame can
differ by a rounding error. A hundredth of a stop is far below any step the app offers
(the smallest is 1/3 EV).

## `BracketSettings`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Bracketing.swift`

```swift
struct BracketSettings
```

Exposure bracketing: several photos at different exposures from one press of the shutter.

Every frame is a separate single-frame photo; nothing is merged (AGENTS.md, "Honesty"). The
frames are taken with the system's manual exposure bracket
(`AVCapturePhotoBracketSettings` with `AVCaptureManualExposureBracketedStillImageSettings`),
so each frame's ISO and shutter are set exactly.

```swift
struct BracketShortcut
```

The parameters of a "Take Bracket" request: the frames, the step between them, their order,
the delay and the lens.

The bracket is taken once, through the same bracket path as the shutter's, whether or not
manual controls are on; the person's own bracket setting doesn't change. Values are moved
to the nearest the app offers (``BracketSettings/frameCounts`` and ``BracketSettings/steps``);
whether the lens can take it is checked when it is taken.

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraIntent.swift`


Turn clean view on or off. Clean view hides every control that isn't essential, leaving

## `CameraIntent/setInForeground(_:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraIntent.swift`

```swift
case setInForeground(Bool)
```

The app came back to the foreground (`true`) or went to the background (`false`). Send it
whenever the scene phase changes to or from the background (issue #64).

Leaving closes the Last Photo viewer and releases the previews in
``CameraModel/sessionPhotos`` (docs/PRIVACY.md), and cancels the self-timer, because the camera stops and can't take its photo. With
the astro helper on, leaving pauses its frames instead (``AstroModel/isPaused``), or, during
its start delay, starts the run paused; coming back with the camera running counts the
start delay down again and continues the run.

- Parameter isInForeground: Whether the app is in the foreground now.

Use the person's photo size for each lens (``NopeSettings/photoSizes``, issue #57). Send
it when the camera screen appears and whenever the choice changes. When the selected
lens's size changes, the camera is reconfigured; a size a lens doesn't offer
(``Lens/photoSizeChoices``) is ignored.

- Parameter sizes: The chosen size for each lens.

## `CameraIntent/setViewerOpen(_:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraIntent.swift`

```swift

```

The Last Photo viewer opened (`true`) or closed (`false`): ``CameraModel/isViewerOpen``.
Send it when the viewer is shown and when it is dismissed. While it is open, a hardware
shutter press closes it and takes no photo (docs/design/BUILD-SPEC.md, 3.14, decision V1).
The interface's Take Photo command (the Space key) does the same by sending
`setViewerOpen(false)` instead of ``capture`` while the viewer is open; ``capture`` itself
still takes a photo, as Siri, Shortcuts and the Apple Watch app do.

- Parameter isOpen: Whether the viewer is open now.

## `CameraModel`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel.swift`

```swift
@MainActor final class CameraModel
```

The camera's state machine: the single object an interface talks to.

An interface observes ``state``, ``selectedLens``, ``captureFormat``, ``sessionStatus`` and
``captureStatus``, and sends ``CameraIntent`` values through ``send(_:)``. It also runs
``observeEvents()`` for as long as it is visible. The model asks its ``CaptureBackend`` for
facts and decides the next state; the interface holds no rules of its own (AGENTS.md, "One
direction of flow").

The model is isolated to the main actor, so an interface can read its properties directly.

## `CameraModel/applyCameraSetup(_:manualControlsOn:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraSetup.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func applyCameraSetup(_ setup: CameraSetup, manualControlsOn: Bool) async -> [PresetNotice]
```

Sets the camera up as a preset describes, through the same intents an interface sends.

The astro helper is turned off first, then the lens is chosen, manual controls are turned
on or off to match the preset's settings, the values are set, and the astro helper is
turned on last, with the preset's values applied after it. Each value goes through the
camera's rules, so a value the lens can't use is moved into its range or left on Auto.

Side effects: may switch the lens, which restarts the camera.

- Parameters:
  - setup: The setup to apply.
  - manualControlsOn: Whether manual controls are on in the preset's settings
    (``NopeSettings/showsManualControls``).
- Returns: What couldn't be applied as saved: ``PresetNotice/cameraNotReady`` when the
  camera isn't on, ``PresetNotice/lensUnavailable`` when the device has no such lens, and
  ``PresetNotice/cameraValuesAdjusted`` when any other value differs from the preset's.

## `CameraModel/applyPreset(_:presets:inbox:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+PresetShortcut.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func applyPreset(_ choice: PresetChoice, presets: SettingsPresets?, inbox: ShortcutInbox) async -> ShortcutOutcome
```

Applies a preset for a request, through the same path as the Presets list
(``SettingsPresets/apply(_:camera:location:)``): every setting, then the camera's lens,
exposure and drive values, with Location only through its permission prompt. A preset
never turns the light on (``CameraSetup`` doesn't hold it). The apply can be undone in the
Presets list, as one made there.

The request waits for the camera to start, so the lens and exposure apply too; if the
camera can't start, the settings still apply and the result says the camera values
didn't (``PresetNotice/cameraNotReady``).

- Parameters:
  - choice: The preset.
  - presets: The person's presets, or `nil` when the interface has none.
  - inbox: The inbox, for its waits and timeouts.
- Returns: ``ShortcutOutcome/presetApplied(_:)``, ``ShortcutOutcome/busy`` while photos
  are being taken, or ``ShortcutOutcome/presetUnavailable`` when the preset doesn't exist
  or can't be read.

## `CameraModel/cameraProblem(inbox:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Shortcuts.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func cameraProblem(inbox: ShortcutInbox) async -> CameraUnavailability?
```

Waits for the camera to finish starting, at most ``ShortcutInbox/startTimeoutSeconds``,
then says why it can't take a photo, if it can't.

- Parameter inbox: The inbox, for its wait and timeout.
- Returns: Why the camera can't take a photo, or `nil` when it can.

nothing while it is false, Siri and Shortcuts refuse a lens change, and presets aren't
applied (``isBusyForPresets``). An interface disables its lens controls while it is false.

interval series or astro run runs, which pressing the shutter stops. Not while a burst's
last photos are being saved: a new burst waits for them (docs/design/BURST.md, decision 9).
In the Interval drive mode, not while the interval is as long as the series' time or
longer (``IntervalBlocker/intervalNotShorterThanTime``): the shutter would start nothing.

## `CameraModel/capture(onceBracket:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func capture(onceBracket: BracketSettings? = nil) async
```

Takes one photo with the selected lens, if the camera can capture now, or the exposure
bracket (``ManualControlsModel/bracket``) when one is on. A photo of an interval series is
always a single photo (docs/design/BRACKETING.md, decision 3).

A blank photo (``CaptureError/blankPicture``) is never kept: the model restarts the
camera once and takes the photo again (`recoverFromBlankPicture(_:settings:)`).

A bracket's frames are planned around the camera's exposure. When the camera has not
reported one yet (just after a lens change, or after manual controls or the astro helper
turned on or off), the shutter waits up to ``bracketReadingWaitSeconds`` for it, then
takes the bracket; if none arrives, it takes nothing and reports
``CaptureError/bracketNotReady``. A bracket never becomes a single photo, and with a
bracket on no photo fires the flash (``shutterUsesFlash``).

- Parameter onceBracket: A bracket to take this time only, through the same bracket path
  as the shutter's, without changing ``ManualControlsModel/bracket`` (a Siri or
  Shortcuts request, issue #62); `nil` for the shutter's usual photo. The caller checks
  first that ``ManualControlsModel/bracketFrames(once:)`` can plan it.

## `CameraModel/forgetNightModeFromAstroAtLaunch(settings:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Astro.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func forgetNightModeFromAstroAtLaunch(settings: NopeSettings)
```

Forgets that the astro helper turned night mode on, without changing night mode, when the
helper ended without leaving it: the app was quit or closed by the system while the helper
was on, and the helper is off at every launch. Night mode stays as it was, so a relaunch in
the dark opens red (owner decision, 2026-11-18); it is the person's from then on. Call it
once the camera screen shows. It does nothing while the helper is on.

Side effects: may store that night mode is no longer the helper's.

- Parameter settings: The person's settings, where the record is stored.

## `CameraModel/holdsBackLevelTap`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Level.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var holdsBackLevelTap: Bool { get }
```

Whether the level's tap is held back now (docs/design/level-haptic.md, section 4): while
a photo is being taken (a bracket and a long exposure included), during a burst or a
self-timer countdown, for the whole of an interval series (running or paused), while Astro
or a focus bracket runs (paused included), and whenever the camera isn't running.

The Taptic Engine moves the device, so a tap is kept away from exposures and series; with
the camera not running there is no live picture to level. An interface also holds the tap
back while a sheet covers the camera or the app isn't active, which the model can't see.

## `CameraModel/init(backend:saver:sleep:now:deviceStatusReader:currentDate:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel.swift`

```swift
@MainActor init(backend: any CaptureBackend, saver: PhotoSaver? = nil, sleep: @escaping @Sendable (Double) async throws -> Void = { try await Task.sleep(for: .seconds($0)) }, now: @escaping @Sendable () -> ContinuousClock.Instant = { .now }, deviceStatusReader: (any DeviceStatusReading)? = nil, currentDate: @escaping @Sendable () -> Date = { Date() })
```

Creates a model in the ``CameraState/starting`` state.

- Parameters:
  - backend: The hardware, or a ``SimulatedCaptureBackend`` in tests and previews.
  - saver: The save pipeline. Without one, every photo ends in
    ``CaptureError/savingUnavailable`` and nothing is saved.
  - sleep: Waits a number of seconds between interval photos and self-timer ticks. Tests
    pass a faster one.
  - now: Reads the clock that times each capture. Tests pass a scripted one.
  - deviceStatusReader: Reads the free storage and battery, or `nil` for no warnings.
  - currentDate: Reads the clock on the wall, for an interval series that stops after a
    time. Tests pass a scripted one.

A ``ManualCaptureBackend`` also gets manual controls (``manual``), and a
``LightCaptureBackend`` a torch (``light``).

default. Warnings that change what the photo is are never hidden.

## `CameraModel/isFinishingPhotos`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+LiveActivity.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var isFinishingPhotos: Bool { get }
```

Whether photos are being taken or saved: a photo, a bracket, a burst or astro frames that
aren't paused. When the app leaves the foreground then, it asks iOS for time to finish
saving them (``BackgroundTimeRequesting``); a paused astro run has saved every frame taken.

## `CameraModel/isFirstStart`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var isFirstStart: Bool { get set }
```

Whether the camera is still on its first start since the model was created: `true` from
creation until the first time the state or the camera's status says how the start went,
then `false` for good.

It ends when ``state`` becomes ``CameraState/needsPermission``,
``CameraState/permissionDenied`` or ``CameraState/unavailable``, or when ``sessionStatus``
becomes anything other than ``SessionStatus/stopped`` or ``SessionStatus/starting``
(running, failed, interrupted, paused for heat, no picture). A later start (a return from
the background, a lens change, a restart of the camera) never makes it `true` again.

An interface shows the launch screen's mark for as long as it is `true`, so the first
frames match the launch screen (docs/design/LAUNCH-SCREEN.md, 6.3); after it, the usual
starting indicator. It is in `NopeCore` so the app, the Lock Screen camera and any other
interface read the first start the same way.

## `CameraModel/liveActivityContent(continuing:isInForeground:now:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+LiveActivity.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func liveActivityContent(continuing kind: LiveActivityContent.Kind?, isInForeground: Bool, now: Date) -> LiveActivityContent?
```

The Live Activity's content for what runs now, or `nil` when nothing runs that it shows.

An interval series, astro frames, a burst and the self-timer each have content while they
run. Out of the foreground the camera stops, so a running interval series or astro run is
``LiveActivityContent/Phase/paused`` (it continues when the app is open again; both are
paused in the foreground too while the camera is), and a running burst is
``LiveActivityContent/Phase/saving`` (the frames already taken are being saved). The self-timer's photo keeps the timer's content while it is
taken and saved.

- Parameters:
  - kind: The kind of the activity already shown, or `nil`.
  - isInForeground: Whether the app is in the foreground.
  - now: The current time, to say when a countdown ends.
- Returns: The content, without night mode (the coordinator adds it).

## `CameraModel/liveActivityOutcome(after:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+LiveActivity.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func liveActivityOutcome(after last: LiveActivityContent) -> LiveActivityContent
```

The final content of an activity whose run has ended: how it ended, how many photos were
saved and whether they are signed.

The model doesn't keep every run's result, so an ended interval series and a self-timer
are judged from the last content shown. That content is current when the app is out of
the foreground, the only time an activity is on screen: the camera has stopped, so nothing
changes unseen.

- Parameter last: The content shown last.
- Returns: The final content.

## `CameraModel/pauseAfterErrorSeconds`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Interval.swift`

```swift
@MainActor static let pauseAfterErrorSeconds: Double
```

How long a photo of an astro run or an interval series that failed waits for a pause, in
seconds, before the failure ends the run (`failureWasAPause()`).

The camera reports a photo cut short by an interruption, and the interruption itself, on
separate paths, and the app hears that it left the foreground from the interface, so the
photo's error can arrive first. One second is an assumption to be checked on a device:
long enough for those events, short enough that a real failure is reported at once.

## `CameraModel/pauseAfterErrorSleep`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var pauseAfterErrorSleep: @Sendable () async throws -> Void
```

Waits ``pauseAfterErrorSeconds`` after a photo of a series fails, for the pause that may
have caused it (`failureWasAPause()`). Tests replace it.

## `CameraModel/pauseAstroRun()`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Astro.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func pauseAstroRun() async -> Bool
```

Pauses the astro run until the app is in the foreground and the camera can take photos
again, then counts the start delay down so the phone is still before the next frame. It
waits for Observation's change notifications; nothing polls (AGENTS.md, "Battery").

- Returns: `false` when the person stopped the run, `true` to continue: the run then
  checks the camera, storage and battery again before the next frame.

## `CameraModel/saveComparing(_:started:takenAt:request:settings:backend:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Comparison.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func saveComparing(_ photo: CapturedPhoto, started: ContinuousClock.Instant, takenAt: Date, request: CaptureRequest, settings: SaveSettings, backend: any ComparisonCaptureBackend) async
```

Takes the phone's version right after the app's photo, saves the app's photo as usual, and
opens the compare view.

Privacy: only the app's photo goes to ``PhotoSaver``. The phone's version is a
``PhoneProcessedPhoto``, which the saver can't take, and it is dropped when the app's photo
fails to save. If the phone's version can't be taken, the app's photo is still saved and
the compare view says why there is nothing to compare.

Side effects: takes a second photo; may show the add-only Photos prompt; adds the app's
photo to Photos.

- Parameters:
  - photo: the app's photo, just taken.
  - started: When the app's photo was started.
  - takenAt: When the shutter took the app's photo, by the device's clock.
  - request: the app's request, which the phone's version repeats.
  - settings: The settings the app's photo is saved with.
  - backend: The backend that takes the phone's version.

## `CameraModel/serveShortcuts(from:settings:presets:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Shortcuts.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func serveShortcuts(from inbox: ShortcutInbox, settings: NopeSettings? = nil, presets: SettingsPresets? = nil) async
```

Performs the requests from the inbox, until the calling task is cancelled: one at a time,
except that an instant request (``ShortcutRequest/isInstant``) runs at once, alongside a
request still in progress. Run it for as long as the camera screen is shown, for example
in a SwiftUI `.task`.

Side effects: as ``CameraIntent/capture`` and ``CameraIntent/selectLens(_:)``; a request
may switch the lens, start the self-timer, take a photo and add it to Photos.

- Parameters:
  - inbox: Where the app's App Intents leave their requests.
  - settings: The person's settings, for requests that change a stored setting of a lens
    (``ShortcutRequest/setPhotoSize(_:)``), or `nil` when the interface stores none; such
    a request then says the lens offers no choice. The interface applies the changed
    setting to the camera, as it does for its own settings screen.
  - presets: The person's settings presets, for ``ShortcutRequest/applyPreset(_:)``, or
    `nil` when the interface has none; such a request then says the preset can't be found.

## `CameraModel/setAstro(_:settings:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Astro.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func setAstro(_ isOn: Bool, settings: NopeSettings) async
```

Turns the helper on or off from an interface's Astro control, with night mode: turning it
on turns night mode on too (issue #60), and turning it off, in any way, returns night mode
to what it was before.

The night mode rule mirrors the one for manual controls:
- Night mode that was off is turned on, and turned off again when the helper ends: Astro
  off, another drive mode, a lens without the helper, manual controls off, or a preset. A
  relaunch keeps night mode as it was (``forgetNightModeFromAstroAtLaunch(settings:)``).
- Night mode that was already on stays on.
- A change the person makes to night mode while the helper is on
  (``NopeSettings/setNightMode(_:)``) is theirs, and is kept.
- With ``NopeSettings/keepsNightModeAfterAstro`` on, night mode stays on.

The helper alone (``CameraIntent/astro(_:)``, as a preset sends it) never changes night
mode: night mode is a setting of the screens, and a preset brings its own.

Side effects: as ``AstroIntent/setOn(_:)``; may turn night mode on and store it. Turning on
is ignored, and night mode left alone, where the helper can't turn on.

- Parameters:
  - isOn: Whether the helper is on.
  - settings: The person's settings, where night mode is stored.

## `CameraModel/setInForeground(_:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Astro.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func setInForeground(_ isInForeground: Bool)
```

Records whether the app is in the foreground, and applies it to the self-timer and the

## `CameraModel/setManualControls(_:settings:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Astro.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func setManualControls(_ isOn: Bool, settings: NopeSettings) async
```

Turns manual controls on or off and stores the choice as the "Manual controls" option
(``NopeSettings/showsManualControls``). Use it for every control that turns manual
controls on or off, such as a Manual Controls button.

Storing the option alone isn't enough: the astro helper turns manual controls on without
changing the option, so storing "off" while the helper had turned them on changes no
stored value, and an interface that follows the option (a SwiftUI `.task(id:)`) sends
nothing. This call stores the option and sends ``ManualControlIntent/setEnabled(_:)``
itself, so turning manual controls off always turns them and the helper off. An interface
that also follows the option may send the same intent again; it changes nothing then.

Side effects: as ``ManualControlIntent/setEnabled(_:)``; off returns every control to
automatic and turns the astro helper off.

- Parameters:
  - isOn: Whether manual controls are on.
  - settings: The person's settings, where the option is stored.

## `CameraModel/showsSteadyHint`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Astro.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var showsSteadyHint: Bool { get }
```

Whether the camera should say "Hold it steady or use a tripod.":
``ManualControlsModel/showsSteadyHint``, except while the astro helper is on. Its long exposures are meant for a tripod, and its
own guidance already says "Use a tripod.", so the hint would only repeat it (the Astro
screens in docs/design draw no hint). Interfaces show the hint from this property.

## `CameraModel/shutterUsesFlash`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Drive.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var shutterUsesFlash: Bool { get }
```

Whether the shutter's photos can fire the flash. Not in Burst (docs/design/BURST.md,
3.1: "No flash in Burst, for one photo or many"), not with a bracket on
(`AVCapturePhotoBracketSettings` takes no flash; ``CaptureRequest/flashMode``), and not
with the astro helper on, whose frames are taken without it as a burst's are
(`takeFrames`).

An interface shows the flash control and its state only while this is `true` (issue #92,
docs/design/SETTING-SCOPE.md, 6). The person's choice (``LightModel/flashMode``) is kept
and is used again in the other modes.

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Drive.swift`

## `CameraModel/takeBurst(frames:start:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Drive.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func takeBurst(frames: Int = DriveModel.maximumBurstFrames, start: BurstStart = .counted) async
```

Takes a burst: single frames as fast as the camera is ready for them, each saved as its
own photo through the same pipeline as any photo (metadata allowlist, read-back and
signing per file). No frame is merged with another (AGENTS.md, "Honesty").

Each frame is saved while the next is taken, so at most two frames are in memory at once
(issue #46, "memory stays bounded"). The burst stops when the person stops it, at
``DriveModel/maximumBurstFrames``, when the next frame wouldn't fit in storage, when the
camera stops, or at the first frame that can't be taken or saved. Every frame taken
before that is still saved; nothing is dropped.

A burst never fires the flash, as brackets don't: the flash can't recharge between rapid
frames.

A held or started burst runs at the session's speed (``burstSpeed``); a counted one
always at Fast, so "as fast as the camera allows" in the Siri action stays true. A burst
at Fast of three photos or more records its rate for the lens, which decides whether Slow
is offered on it (``DriveModel/offersSlowBurst(on:)``).

Side effects: may show the add-only Photos prompt; adds each photo to Photos.

- Parameters:
  - frames: The most frames to take: ``DriveModel/maximumBurstFrames`` for a held or
    started burst, 1 for a press that reports no phases, or the count a Siri or Shortcuts
    request asked for (issue #62).
  - start: Who begins the burst.

## `CameraModel/takeFocusBracket(_:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+FocusBracket.swift`

```swift
@discardableResult @MainActor func takeFocusBracket(_ settings: FocusBracketSettings) async -> Bool
```

Takes a focus bracket now: for each position, lock focus there and wait for the system's
handler, read back the position, take one photo, save it, go on.

Side effects: may show the add-only Photos prompt; adds each photo to Photos; sets the
camera's focus, exposure and white balance while it runs and restores them afterwards.

- Parameter settings: The bracket.
- Returns: Whether the bracket began: `false` when no photo can be taken, the lens's focus
  can't be set or there is no save pipeline.

## `CameraModel/takeFrames(limit:tracker:stopsOnLowBattery:endAtLimit:pausesWithCamera:minimumInterval:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Drive.swift`

```swift
@discardableResult @MainActor func takeFrames(limit: Int, tracker: any FrameRunTracking, stopsOnLowBattery: Bool, endAtLimit: BurstEnd = .limitReached, pausesWithCamera: Bool = false, minimumInterval: Duration? = nil) async -> Bool
```

Takes up to `limit` single frames back to back, each saved as its own photo while the
next is taken: the loop behind a burst and an astro run (``AstroModel``). See
`takeBurst()` for how frames are saved and when the run stops. Each frame carries the
location when the Location switch is on, as a single photo does.

- Parameters:
  - limit: The most frames to take.
  - tracker: What the run reports its progress to.
  - stopsOnLowBattery: Whether the run also stops when the battery is at or below
    ``AstroModel/stopBatteryLevel`` and not charging.
  - endAtLimit: What the run reports when it takes all `limit` frames.
  - pausesWithCamera: Whether the run pauses instead of stopping when the app leaves the
    foreground or the camera pauses, and continues afterwards (the astro helper, issue
    #64; see `pauseAstroRun()`). A frame cut short by the pause is taken again, also
    when its error arrives before the pause does (`failureWasAPause()`).
  - minimumInterval: The least time between the starts of two frames (a Slow burst), or
    `nil` to take each as soon as the camera is ready. The run waits with the drive's
    sleep and checks again whether to stop after the wait.
- Returns: Whether the run began: `false` when no photo could be taken, the camera has no
  lens or there is no save pipeline, and the tracker was not told of a run.

Side effects: may show the add-only Photos prompt; adds each photo to Photos.

## `CameraModel/takeIntervalPhoto()`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Interval.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func takeIntervalPhoto() async -> Bool
```

Takes one photo of the running interval series, the way the shutter does. While the
series should pause (``seriesShouldPause``) it waits, marked as paused, instead of
skipping the photo; it also waits while another photo is being taken or saved (such as
the photo the shutter took just before the series started) or the compare view is open.
A photo cut short by a pause is taken again after it.

- Returns: Whether the photo was taken and saved. `false` ends the series: it was stopped,
  the camera stopped for good, or the photo failed for another reason (the camera says
  why).

## `CameraModel/watchStatus`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Watch.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var watchStatus: WatchStatus { get }
```

What the camera is doing, as the watch shows it. It reads only observable state, so a
``WatchCommandServer`` can follow it without polling.

A camera that is still starting counts as ``WatchCameraProblem/notRunning``: a photo is
taken for the watch only when the camera is already running, so nothing is taken later by
surprise (issue #63, "Done when"). Busy follows the same rule as a Siri request: a photo
being taken or saved, or a countdown, burst, interval series or astro
run.

## `CameraPreviewSource`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraPreviewSource.swift`

```swift
protocol CameraPreviewSource : AnyObject, Sendable
```

An opaque handle to the live camera feed, for the view that shows the viewfinder.

`NopeCore` can't show video: it imports only Foundation. A ``CaptureBackend`` hands out a
preview source through ``CaptureBackend/previewSource``, and the app target, which knows the
concrete type, turns it into a view (for the AVFoundation backend, a view backed by
`AVCaptureVideoPreviewLayer`). An interface receives that view from the app and never looks
inside the handle (AGENTS.md, "Architecture: interface separate from logic").

The live preview is processed by the system; it is a framing guide, not the photo.

## `CameraPreviewSource/devicePoint(forViewfinderPoint:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraPreviewSource.swift`


Returns the point unchanged: a source without video has no rotation or mirroring.

- Parameter point: The point in the viewfinder.
- Returns: `point`.

## `CameraSetup`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraSetup.swift`

```swift
struct CameraSetup
```

The camera's own state in a settings preset (issue #72): the lens, the manual exposure values,
Close-Up, the drive mode, bracketing, the interval series and the astro helper.

These values belong to ``CameraModel``, not to ``NopeSettings``: the app doesn't remember them
between launches (AGENTS.md, "Cognitive": no surprise modes), so a preset keeps them itself.
``CameraModel/cameraSetup`` reads them and ``CameraModel/applyCameraSetup(_:manualControlsOn:)``
sets them again through the same intents an interface sends, so every rule of the camera
(lens ranges, exposure modes, what a lens supports) still applies.

Left out on purpose, because they belong to one moment and one scene rather than to a setup:
the torch being on (the light always starts off, issue #38), a tapped focus point and the
focus and exposure locks, the focus magnifier, clean view while the camera is open (its
setting, "Start in Clean View", is in ``NopeSettings``) and the comparison shot.

The encoding is forward compatible: every value is optional when decoding, and a value that is
missing (a preset saved by an earlier version) or not understood (a choice added by a later
version) is read as its ``factory`` value.

## `CameraStatsReading/init(iso:exposureDuration:exposureBias:exposureOffset:aperture:exposureMode:lensPosition:focusMode:isAdjustingFocus:focusRange:whiteBalance:gains:whiteBalanceMode:zoomFactor:processingOn:isReadyToCapture:systemPressure:isLowPowerMode:torchLevel:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraStats.swift`

```swift
init(iso: Double, exposureDuration: Double, exposureBias: Double, exposureOffset: Double, aperture: Double, exposureMode: ExposureModeReading, lensPosition: Double, focusMode: FocusModeReading, isAdjustingFocus: Bool, focusRange: FocusRangeReading?, whiteBalance: WhiteBalance?, gains: WhiteBalanceGainsReading, whiteBalanceMode: WhiteBalanceModeReading, zoomFactor: Double, processingOn: Set<ProcessingFeature>, isReadyToCapture: Bool, systemPressure: SystemPressureReading, isLowPowerMode: Bool, torchLevel: Double?)
```

Creates a reading.

- Parameters:
  - iso: The ISO in use.
  - exposureDuration: The exposure duration, in seconds.
  - exposureBias: The exposure bias, in EV.
  - exposureOffset: The metered offset, in EV.
  - aperture: The aperture, as an f-number.
  - exposureMode: How exposure is controlled.
  - lensPosition: The relative focus position, 0 to 1.
  - focusMode: How focus is controlled.
  - isAdjustingFocus: Whether the lens is moving to focus.
  - focusRange: The autofocus range restriction, or `nil` when unsupported.
  - whiteBalance: The white balance, or `nil`.
  - gains: The white balance gains.
  - whiteBalanceMode: How white balance is controlled.
  - zoomFactor: The zoom factor.
  - processingOn: The processing features that are on.
  - isReadyToCapture: Whether a photo can be taken now.
  - systemPressure: The pressure level.
  - isLowPowerMode: Whether Low Power Mode is on.
  - torchLevel: The torch level, or `nil` without a torch.

## `CaptureBackend`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureBackend.swift`

```swift
protocol CaptureBackend : Sendable
```

The hardware side of the camera, as seen by ``CameraModel``.

`NopeCapture` implements it with AVFoundation on a device. Tests, previews and the
development build use ``SimulatedCaptureBackend``. Keeping the hardware behind this protocol
lets every rule in `NopeCore` be tested without a camera (AGENTS.md, "Full test coverage").

A backend never decides formats or policies: ``CameraModel`` sends it a ``CaptureRequest``
that says which lens and which format to use.

## `CaptureBackend/capturePhoto(_:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureBackend.swift`

```swift
func capturePhoto(_ request: CaptureRequest) async throws(CaptureError) -> CapturedPhoto
```

Takes one photo and returns it in memory.

Side effects: none beyond the capture itself. The photo isn't written to a file or saved.

- Parameter request: The lens and format; the same as the last ``startSession(_:)``.
- Returns: The photo's file bytes and capture details.
- Throws: ``CaptureError/notRunning`` if the camera isn't running, or
  ``CaptureError/captureFailed`` if the system reports an error or returns no file.

## `CaptureBackend/currentPermission()`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureBackend.swift`

```swift
func currentPermission() async -> CameraPermission
```

Returns the current camera permission without asking the person.

- Returns: The permission the system reports right now.

## `CaptureBackend/deviceCapabilities()`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureBackend.swift`

```swift
func deviceCapabilities() async -> DeviceCapabilities
```

Discovers the device's cameras and what each one supports.

Call it only after access is ``CameraPermission/authorized``.

- Returns: The capabilities of this device.

released.


Stops the camera. Does nothing if it isn't running.

## `CaptureError`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureError.swift`

```swift
enum CaptureError
```

Why the camera couldn't start or couldn't take a photo.

Each case says how an interface should present it. None of them is the person's fault, and
none asks them to change a setting.

## `CaptureError/blankPicture`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureError.swift`

```swift
case blankPicture
```

The camera delivered a photo whose pixels are all one value (a zero-filled buffer): it
is running and sending frames, but no picture. The photo is discarded.

``CameraModel`` restarts the camera once and retries; if the retry is blank too, it moves
to ``SessionStatus/noPicture``. Say that no photo was taken because the camera sent a
blank picture.

## `CaptureError/bracketNotReady`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureError.swift`

```swift
case bracketNotReady
```

A bracket was asked for, but the camera reported no exposure to plan its frames around
within ``CameraModel/bracketReadingWaitSeconds`` (just after a lens change, or after
manual controls or the astro helper turned on or off), or the lens can't take it. No
photo was taken: a bracket never becomes a single photo.

Say that no photos were taken because the camera wasn't ready for the bracket, and that
the person can try again.

## `CaptureError/captureFailed`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureError.swift`

```swift
case captureFailed
```

The system reported an error while taking the photo, or returned no file.

Say that the photo wasn't taken and that the person can try again.

## `CaptureError/configurationFailed`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureError.swift`

```swift
case configurationFailed
```

The selected lens couldn't be opened or configured.

Say that the camera couldn't start, and offer to try another lens.

## `CaptureError/developmentFailed`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureError.swift`

```swift
case developmentFailed
```

A HEIF or JPEG couldn't be developed from the RAW, so nothing was saved.

Say that the photo wasn't saved and that the person can try again.

## `CaptureError/metadataNotAllowed`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureError.swift`

```swift
case metadataNotAllowed
```

A finished file failed the read-back: it held a tag, block or value that isn't on the
metadata allowlist, its RAW image data changed, or it couldn't be read. Nothing was
saved (AGENTS.md, "Fail closed").

Say plainly that the photo wasn't saved because its details couldn't be confirmed as
only the ones the person chose.

## `CaptureError/notRunning`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureError.swift`

```swift
case notRunning
```

The camera isn't running, for example during an interruption.

Say that no photo was taken because the camera is paused.

## `CaptureError/photosAccessDenied`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureError.swift`

```swift
case photosAccessDenied
```

The app has no permission to add photos.

Say that the photo wasn't saved, and that adding photos can be allowed in Settings.

## `CaptureError/saveFailed`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureError.swift`

```swift
case saveFailed
```

Photos reported an error while adding the photo.

Say that the photo wasn't saved and that the person can try again.

## `CaptureError/savingUnavailable`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureError.swift`

```swift
case savingUnavailable
```

This build of the app has no way to save photos (``CameraModel`` was created without a
``PhotoSaver``, as in previews and some tests).

Say that the photo wasn't saved.

```swift
enum CaptureEvent
```

``CaptureBackend`` through ``CaptureBackend/events``.

``CameraModel`` turns events into ``SessionStatus`` values; interfaces never read events.

```swift
case failed
```


The system paused the camera.


The system resumed the camera after an interruption.


The scene changed substantially, for example the camera moved
(`AVCaptureDevice.subjectAreaDidChangeNotification`). Sent only while a tapped point is


## `CaptureFormat`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureFormat.swift`

```swift
enum CaptureFormat
```

What one capture produces: single-frame Bayer RAW, or the labeled processed fallback.

``CameraModel`` chooses it with ``init(for:)``; interfaces only display it (AGENTS.md, "One
direction of flow"). Apple ProRAW is never used: it is demosaiced and may combine frames
(AGENTS.md, "Honesty").

## `CaptureFormat/bayerRAW(pixelFormat:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CaptureFormat.swift`

```swift
case bayerRAW(pixelFormat: String)
```

Single-frame Bayer RAW, saved as DNG.

- Parameter pixelFormat: The four-character code of the Bayer pixel format, such as

## `ChangeWaiter/wait(timeoutSeconds:sleep:until:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ShortcutInbox.swift`

```swift
@MainActor static func wait(timeoutSeconds: Double?, sleep: @escaping @Sendable (Double) async throws -> Void, until condition: @escaping @MainActor () -> Bool) async -> Bool
```

Waits, without polling, until the condition holds, or until the timeout passes.

The condition is checked again each time a value it read changes (Observation,
`withObservationTracking`), so it must read only observable state.

- Parameters:
  - timeoutSeconds: The longest wait in seconds, or `nil` to wait until it holds.
  - sleep: Waits a number of seconds, for the timeout.
  - condition: What to wait for.
- Returns: Whether the condition holds at the end.

## `CloseUpDetector`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AutoControls.swift`

```swift
struct CloseUpDetector
```

Notices when the subject seems closer than the lens can focus: the lens stays at its nearest
position while autofocus keeps searching (issue #51, "Close-up assist").

It only suggests the ultra wide lens; the app never switches lenses on its own. The thresholds
are assumptions to check on the device (docs/TESTING.md, "Close-up focus aids"):
`lensPosition` is relative (0 is the nearest the lens can focus), not a distance.

## `ComparisonCaptureBackend`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Comparison.swift`

```swift
protocol ComparisonCaptureBackend : CaptureBackend
```

A ``CaptureBackend`` that can also take the phone's fully processed photo for the comparison
shot (issue #55).

`NopeCapture` implements it with AVFoundation: a second capture, right after the app's own,
with `.quality` prioritization, which lets the system combine several frames where the
device does that. iOS doesn't allow that processing in the same capture as a Bayer RAW,
so it is a separate photo a moment later. ``SimulatedCaptureBackend`` returns a placeholder.

## `ComparisonCaptureBackend/captureComparison(_:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Comparison.swift`

```swift
func captureComparison(_ request: CaptureRequest) async throws(CaptureError) -> PhoneProcessedPhoto
```

Takes the phone's fully processed photo with the request's lens, in memory only.

Side effects: none beyond the capture itself. The photo isn't written to a file or saved,
and the camera is left configured for the app's own photos afterwards.

- Parameter request: The lens, format and flash of the app's photo, just taken; the same as
  the last ``CaptureBackend/startSession(_:)``. Its metadata plan is ignored: the
  phone's version keeps the system's own metadata, because that is what it shows.
- Returns: The photo.
- Throws: ``CaptureError/notRunning`` if the camera isn't running, or
  ``CaptureError/captureFailed`` if the system reports an error or returns no file.

```swift
case close
```

Close the compare view and discard the phone's version and both pictures.

## `ComparisonIntent/setOn(_:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Comparison.swift`

```swift
case setOn(Bool)
```

Turn the comparison shot on or off. While it is on, each single or self-timer photo is
followed by the phone's fully processed photo, and the compare view opens after the app's
photo is saved. Ignored when the backend can't take the second photo
(``ComparisonModel/isAvailable``). Not remembered between launches.

## `ComparisonModel`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Comparison.swift`

```swift
@MainActor final class ComparisonModel
```

The comparison shot (issue #55): whether it is on, and the compare view's content.

``CameraModel/comparison`` holds it; its intents arrive as ``CameraIntent/comparison(_:)``.
While ``isOn``, ``CameraModel`` follows each single or self-timer photo with the phone's
fully processed photo (``ComparisonCaptureBackend``), saves only the app's photo through
``PhotoSaver`` as usual, and then fills ``state`` for the compare view.

Privacy: the phone's version never reaches the save pipeline. Its pictures and metadata are
kept in ``state`` only while the compare view is open; ``ComparisonIntent/close`` drops
them. Nothing is written to disk, logged or sent anywhere.

```swift
@MainActor func present(_ phone: Result<PhoneProcessedPhoto, CaptureError>, saved: SavedPhoto, files: [PhotoFile], delay: Duration, mirrorsPhoneImage: Bool) async
```

Opens the compare view after the app's photo was saved: the failure when the phone's
version couldn't be taken, otherwise both pictures and the metadata side by side.

The pictures are decoded off the main actor. If the view was closed meanwhile, the result
is dropped.

## `ComparisonModel/renderer`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Comparison.swift`

```swift

```

Decodes both photos into pictures for the compare view, or `nil` for none: the comparison

`ImageIOThumbnailer` with a large size does it with ImageIO, in memory. The Lock Screen
capture extension sets none, so the comparison shot isn't offered on a locked device,
where an extension's memory is limited and photos shouldn't be shown at full size.

## `ControlScale`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ControlScale.swift`

```swift
struct ControlScale
```

The values one manual control steps through on a lens, smallest first.

Interfaces draw a ruler or slider over ``values`` and send the chosen value; VoiceOver's
adjustable action sends ``ManualControlIntent/step(_:by:)``, which uses ``value(from:steppedBy:)``.
Every scale ends exactly at the lens's limits, so the whole range the hardware reports is
reachable.

## `ControlScale/scale(for:on:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ControlScale.swift`

```swift

```

The scale for a control on a lens, or `nil` when the lens reports no range for it.

- ISO and shutter: the one-third-stop marked values within the lens's range, plus its
  limits.
- Exposure compensation: thirds of a stop within ±3 EV and the lens's range.
- Aperture: the f-numbers the system recommends.
- Focus: twenty steps from 0 (closest) to 1 (far).
- White balance: the temperature in 100 K steps over ``WhiteBalance/temperatureRange``.

- Parameters:
  - control: The control.
  - lens: The lens.
- Returns: The scale, or `nil`.

## `Countdown`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/SelfTimer.swift`

```swift
@MainActor final class Countdown
```

A countdown in whole seconds that runs an action when it reaches zero: the self-timer.

``CameraModel`` owns one through ``DriveModel/countdown``. The shutter starts it in a timer
drive mode (``DriveMode/timer(_:)``), and ``DriveIntent/startTimer(seconds:)`` starts it with
any number of seconds, for example from a Siri or Shortcuts request with a delay (issue #62).
An interface shows and announces ``remainingSeconds`` and gives a haptic tick when it changes.

Battery rule (AGENTS.md, "Battery"): the countdown only sleeps between ticks; it does no
other work, and nothing keeps running once it ends or is cancelled.

```swift
countdown.start(seconds: 3) { await takePhoto() }
countdown.remainingSeconds  // 3, then 2, then 1, then nil when the photo is taken
```

## `Countdown/cancel()`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/SelfTimer.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func cancel()
```

Stops the countdown without running its action. Does nothing when none runs.

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/SelfTimer.swift`

```swift
@MainActor init(sleep: @escaping @Sendable (Double) async throws -> Void)
```

Creates a countdown that isn't running.

- Parameter sleep: Waits a number of seconds between ticks, throwing when cancelled.

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/SelfTimer.swift`

```swift

```

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/SelfTimer.swift`

```swift

```

## `Countdown/run(seconds:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/SelfTimer.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func run(seconds: Int) async -> Bool
```

Counts down and waits until the count reaches zero or the countdown is cancelled: the
astro helper's start delay before it resumes a paused run (issue #64).

- Parameter seconds: The delay, in seconds. Less than 1 doesn't wait.
- Returns: `true` when the count reached zero, `false` when the countdown was cancelled or
  another countdown was already running.

## `Countdown/start(seconds:fire:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/SelfTimer.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func start(seconds: Int, fire: @escaping @MainActor () async -> Void)
```

Starts counting down, one tick a second, and runs the action when the count reaches zero.
Does nothing when a countdown already runs or `seconds` is less than 1.

- Parameters:
  - seconds: The delay, in seconds.
  - fire: What happens at zero, such as taking a photo. It doesn't run when the countdown
    is cancelled.

## `DarwinNotificationListeners`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/SharedSettings.swift`

```swift
final class DarwinNotificationListeners
```

The listeners for Darwin notifications in this process, by name.

A Core Foundation callback is a C function that can't capture a listener, so one callback per
name forwards to the streams registered here. The callback stays registered for the life of the
process; a stream that ends is removed.

## `DevelopedFileOptions`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/DevelopedFileOptions.swift`

```swift
struct DevelopedFileOptions
```

How the HEIF and JPEG copies the app develops from the RAW are encoded: compression, HEIF bit
depth, color space and size (issue #57, "File").

Owner principle (issue #57): anything about the file that the device lets an app control is
the person's to choose, behind the advanced options, with the defaults working on their own.
The defaults are the encoding the app has always used: HEIF at the system encoder's own
quality and 10 bits per channel, JPEG at maximum quality, both in Display P3. None of the
options change the RAW, the metadata or the signature, and every combination is encoded and
opened again by the unit tests (`NopeDevelopTests`), because a setting that makes a file
that doesn't open isn't offered.

``SaveSettings/fileOptions`` holds the choice; `NopeDevelop` turns it into ImageIO options.

## `DevelopedLook`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/DevelopedLook.swift`

```swift
enum DevelopedLook
```

How the app develops the HEIF and JPEG it makes from the RAW. The RAW (DNG) itself is never
affected.

Both looks demosaic the sensor data and apply the file's own white balance and color
matrices, with no noise reduction, sharpening, local contrast, local tone mapping or lens
correction (`docs/PRIVACY.md`, "How each file's metadata is built and checked"; owner
decision, 2026-11-12). They differ only in the tone curve.

``SaveSettings/developedLook`` holds the choice; a Settings screen shows it as a picker.

## `DevelopedLook/flat`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/DevelopedLook.swift`

```swift
case flat
```

No tone curve: the image is linear to the scene, and only the output color space's
standard transfer function is applied when it is encoded, so it looks flat and low in
contrast. Meant for editing afterwards.

## `DevelopedLook/standard`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/DevelopedLook.swift`

```swift
case standard
```

One global tone curve, the RAW developer's default for the camera, so the photo looks
like a normal photo. The default.

## `DeviceControls/init(exposure:focus:whiteBalance:metering:pointOfInterest:nearFocusLimit:longestAutoExposure:farFocusLimit:followsFaces:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/DeviceControls.swift`

```swift
init(exposure: Exposure, focus: Focus, whiteBalance: WhiteBalanceControl, metering: MeteringMode, pointOfInterest: PointOfInterest? = nil, nearFocusLimit: Bool = false, longestAutoExposure: Double? = nil, farFocusLimit: Bool = false, followsFaces: Bool = true)
```

Creates controls from their parts. ``ManualControlsModel`` uses ``init(settings:lens:assist:)``.

- Parameters:
  - exposure: The exposure.
  - focus: The focus.
  - whiteBalance: The white balance.
  - metering: The metering mode.
  - pointOfInterest: The tapped point, or `nil`. Defaults to `nil`.
  - nearFocusLimit: Whether autofocus is limited to near subjects. Defaults to `false`.
  - longestAutoExposure: The longest auto exposure in seconds, or `nil`. Defaults to `nil`.
  - farFocusLimit: Whether autofocus is limited to far subjects. Defaults to `false`;
    ignored while `nearFocusLimit` is on.
  - followsFaces: Whether detected faces steer autofocus and auto exposure. Defaults to
    `true`.

## `DeviceControls/init(settings:lens:assist:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/DeviceControls.swift`

```swift
init(settings: ManualSettings, lens: Lens, assist: AssistSettings = .none)
```

Builds the controls for settings on a lens.

- Values outside the lens's ranges are moved to the nearest limit.
- A control the lens doesn't support stays automatic.
- An exposure combination the lens doesn't support (for example shutter priority before
  iOS 27) falls back to auto exposure; ``ManualControlsModel`` never asks for one.
- Exposure compensation is dropped when ISO and shutter are both set, because the camera
  has nothing left to adjust (`AVCaptureDevice.exposureTargetBias`).

- The automatic aids apply only to what is automatic: the tapped point aims autofocus
  only while focus is automatic, and auto exposure only while exposure is automatic; the
  near and far focus limits need autofocus; the longest shutter needs auto exposure. Each
  needs the lens to support it. Face detection is passed on as it is; the backend applies
  it to whatever is automatic.

- Parameters:
  - settings: The person's settings.
  - lens: The selected lens.
  - assist: The automatic aids. Defaults to none.


The device's free storage and battery, for the camera's warnings and to stop a burst before
storage runs out (issue #44).

``CameraModel`` owns one as ``CameraModel/deviceStatus``. Nothing polls: the model reads it
when the camera starts, before each photo and after each save, and the app calls
``refresh()`` when the system reports a battery or Low Power Mode change (AGENTS.md,
"Battery").

only when the battery is nearly empty. An astro run stops earlier, at ``AstroModel/stopBatteryLevel``.

## `DeviceStatusReading`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/DeviceStatus.swift`

```swift
@MainActor protocol DeviceStatusReading
```

Reads the device's free storage and battery state. The app implements it with the system
APIs; tests and the UI tests pass fakes.

Privacy rule (AGENTS.md, "Nothing tracks you"): the values are read on the device, used only
for the camera's warnings and never stored or sent. The free storage API is a required-reason
API, declared in the app's privacy manifest.

## `DistortionCorrection`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/DistortionCorrection.swift`

```swift
enum DistortionCorrection
```

Whether a lens lets the app turn off the camera's geometric distortion correction, as the
camera reports it (issue #80).

Geometric distortion correction reshapes the picture to lessen a wide lens's fisheye effect,
so it changes pixels. The app turns it off wherever the camera allows (`docs/PRIVACY.md`,
"Processing"). Apple's documentation says "RAW photo captures never have GDC applied,
regardless of the value of AVCaptureDevice.geometricDistortionCorrectionEnabled"
(`AVCaptureDevice.h`, `geometricDistortionCorrectionSupported`), so this matters only for a
lens's processed fallback. An interface discloses it in the lens's processing note and in
the photo's details.

The value is what the camera reports, not a measurement: whether a lens that offers no
switch corrects its photos anyway is checked on real devices (`docs/TESTING.md`).

## `DistortionCorrection/notSwitchable`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/DistortionCorrection.swift`

```swift
case notSwitchable
```

The camera offers apps no switch for the correction
(`AVCaptureDevice.isGeometricDistortionCorrectionSupported` is `false`) and doesn't report
whether its processed photos are corrected, so they may be.

## `DistortionCorrection/turnedOff`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/DistortionCorrection.swift`

```swift
case turnedOff
```

The camera lets apps turn the correction off
(`AVCaptureDevice.isGeometricDistortionCorrectionSupported` is `true`), and the app turns
it off.

## `DriveModel`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/DriveMode.swift`

```swift
@MainActor final class DriveModel
```

The drive mode and the state of the self-timer and of a burst.

``CameraModel`` owns one as ``CameraModel/drive``. An interface reads it and sends
``DriveIntent`` values as ``CameraIntent/drive(_:)``; the shutter itself sends
``CameraIntent/capture``, and the model applies the mode. Single is the default, and the mode
isn't stored: every launch starts in single (AGENTS.md, "Cognitive": no surprise modes).

## `FastShutter`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AutoControls.swift`

```swift
enum FastShutter
```

How fast a shutter full auto prefers (issue #56). The camera's auto exposure still chooses
the shutter and ISO; the app only sets the longest shutter it may use
(`AVCaptureDevice.activeMaxExposureDuration`), so it raises ISO instead of slowing the
shutter. Manual controls are never changed by it.

```swift

```

At least 1/1000 s, to freeze fast motion such as insects or sports.

## `FastShutter/freezeMotion`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AutoControls.swift`

```swift

```

At least 1/500 s, to freeze people and pets in motion.

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AutoControls.swift`

```swift
case handheld
```

At least 1/(2 × the lens's 35 mm-equivalent focal length), a common rule for a sharp
handheld photo. The default.

When the flash fires for a photo (issue #38).

The flash only adds light; the app turns on no processing with it. Each lens lists the modes
it supports in ``Lens/flashModes``; an interface offers a flash control only when that list
has a mode other than ``off``.

The camera decides at capture time whether the scene needs the flash.

The flash never fires. The default.


The flash fires for every photo, unless the device is too hot for it (the system then
takes the photo without it).

## `FocusBracketSettings`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/BracketModel.swift`

```swift
struct FocusBracketSettings
```

The settings of a focus bracket: a series of separate single-frame photos, each at the next
focus position, for stacking in other software (docs/design/BRACKETING.md, section 5).

Positions are on the lens's own relative scale (`AVCaptureDevice.lensPosition`): 0 is the
closest the lens focuses and 1 the farthest. Apple documents that a position "does not
correspond to an exact physical distance", so the app never turns one into a distance.

## `FocusBracketSettings/defaultFrameCount`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/BracketModel.swift`

```swift
static let defaultFrameCount: Int
```

The frame count a focus bracket starts with.

## `FocusBracketSettings/farthest`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/BracketModel.swift`

```swift
let farthest: Double
```

The farthest focus position, from 0 to 1, never below ``nearest``.

## `FocusBracketSettings/frameCount`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/BracketModel.swift`

```swift
let frameCount: Int
```

The number of frames, one of ``frameCounts``.

## `FocusBracketSettings/frameCounts`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/BracketModel.swift`

```swift
static let frameCounts: [Int]
```

The frame counts the app offers. 50 is the most one burst takes
(``DriveModel/maximumBurstFrames``; decision 12).

## `FocusBracketSettings/init(nearest:farthest:frameCount:order:returnsFocus:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/BracketModel.swift`

```swift
init(nearest: Double, farthest: Double, frameCount: Int, order: FocusBracketOrder = .nearestFirst, returnsFocus: Bool = true)
```

Creates focus bracket settings. Each position is moved into 0…1; a nearest position
beyond the farthest swaps the two (docs/design/BRACKETING.md, section 5); a frame count
that isn't offered becomes the nearest one that is.

- Parameters:
  - nearest: The nearest focus position, from 0 to 1.
  - farthest: The farthest focus position, from 0 to 1.
  - frameCount: The number of frames.
  - order: The order of the frames.
  - returnsFocus: Whether the focus returns to what it was when the bracket ends.


- Returns: `true` when the lens supports manual focus.

## `FramePoint`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/FramePoint.swift`

```swift
struct FramePoint
```

A point in a picture, in normalized coordinates: (0, 0) is the top-left corner and (1, 1) the
bottom-right corner.

An interface sends a point in the viewfinder's own coordinates (where the person tapped,
divided by the viewfinder's size) with ``AutoControlIntent/focusAndExpose(at:)``.
``CameraModel`` turns it into the camera's coordinates with
``CameraPreviewSource/devicePoint(forViewfinderPoint:)`` before it reaches the hardware
(`focusPointOfInterest` and `exposurePointOfInterest` use the same 0-to-1 range).

## `FramePoint/center`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/FramePoint.swift`

```swift
static let center: FramePoint
```

The middle of the picture.

## `FramePoint/init(x:y:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/FramePoint.swift`

```swift
init(x: Double, y: Double)
```

Creates a point, moving each coordinate inside 0 through 1, so a tap at the very edge
(or a rounding error just past it) is still a valid point of interest.

- Parameters:
  - x: The horizontal position, 0 to 1.
  - y: The vertical position, 0 to 1.

## `FramePoint/normalized(locationX:locationY:width:height:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/FramePoint.swift`

```swift
static func normalized(locationX: Double, locationY: Double, width: Double, height: Double) -> FramePoint?
```

The point for a location inside a frame of a size, for example a tap in the viewfinder.

- Parameters:
  - locationX: The horizontal location, in points from the left edge.
  - locationY: The vertical location, in points from the top edge.
  - width: The frame's width, in points.
  - height: The frame's height, in points.
- Returns: The normalized point, or `nil` for an empty frame.

## `FramePoint/unmagnified(scale:anchor:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/AutoControls.swift`

```swift
func unmagnified(scale: Double, anchor: FramePoint) -> FramePoint
```

The point in the unmagnified viewfinder under a point in the magnified one.

The focus magnifier draws the viewfinder scaled around an anchor; a tap on it lands on
`anchor + (point − anchor) / scale` of the real viewfinder.

- Parameters:
  - scale: The magnification, greater than 0.
  - anchor: The point the magnifier is centered on.
- Returns: The point in the unmagnified viewfinder.

## `GravitySource`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Level.swift`

```swift
protocol GravitySource : Sendable
```

Where the level's gravity readings come from: Core Motion on a device, a fixed reading in UI
tests. The app target supplies it; `NopeCore` doesn't import Core Motion.

Battery rule (AGENTS.md, "Battery"): motion updates run only while a stream from
``gravityUpdates()`` is being read, that is while the level is shown, and stop when it ends.

## `GravitySource/gravityUpdates()`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Level.swift`

```swift
func gravityUpdates() -> AsyncStream<GravityVector>
```

Starts motion updates and returns them as a stream. The updates stop when the stream is
no longer read (its task is cancelled) or the stream ends.

- Returns: Gravity readings, a few dozen a second at most. The stream ends at once on a
  device without motion sensors.

## `GravityVector`

```swift
struct GravityVector
```

The direction of gravity in the device's own axes, in units of g, as Core Motion reports it
(`CMDeviceMotion.gravity`): x toward the right edge of the screen, y toward its top edge, and
z out of the screen toward the person. A device held upright in portrait reads about
(0, −1, 0); lying flat, screen up, about (0, 0, −1).

## `HEIFReader`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/HEIFReader.swift`

```swift
struct HEIFReader
```

Lists every box, item, item property and item reference of a HEIF file, and reads the tags
in its EXIF item and the properties in its XMP item.

Read-only, bounds-checked and fuzz-tested, like ``TIFFReader``. Written from ISO/IEC 14496-12
(ISOBMFF: box headers 4.2, `meta` 8.11.1, `iloc` 8.11.3, `iinf`/`infe` 8.11.6, `iref`
8.11.12, `idat` 8.11.11) and ISO/IEC 23008-12 (HEIF: `iprp`/`ipco`/`ipma` 9.3, the `Exif`
item and its TIFF header offset in annex A.2.1, XMP as a `mime` item in A.2.2).

Block names:
- `heif:box:<type>` for each top-level box;
- `heif:meta:<type>` for each box inside `meta`;
- `heif:item:<type>` for each item, and `heif:item:mime:<content type>` for a MIME item;
- `heif:property:<type>` for each item property;
- `heif:reference:<type>` for each item reference type.

## `HEIFReader/integer(in:at:size:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/HEIFReader.swift`

```swift
static func integer(in buffer: [UInt8], at position: Int, size: Int) -> Int?
```

A big-endian unsigned integer of 0 to 8 bytes in a buffer, or `nil` past the end or when
it is larger than an eighth of `Int.max`, so sums of a few of them can't overflow.

- Parameters:
  - buffer: The bytes.
  - position: The first byte.
  - size: The number of bytes.

```swift
static let maximumBoxes: Int
```

The most boxes the reader reads before giving up.

## `IntervalSettings`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Bracketing.swift`

```swift
struct IntervalSettings
```

Interval shooting: a series of separate photos at a fixed interval, until a number of photos
is taken or a time is up (``StopAfter``).

Each photo is an ordinary single capture; nothing is combined into a video or merged. The
timer runs only while a series is running and the app is open (AGENTS.md, "Battery").

## `IntervalSettings/StopAfter/time`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Bracketing.swift`


The series ends once ``IntervalSettings/duration`` has passed since it started, by the
clock on the wall: the time keeps running while the series is paused, so a series of
one hour started at 8:41 PM ends at 9:41 PM (``IntervalProgress/timeSpan``).
