## `JPEGReader`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/JPEGReader.swift`

```swift
enum JPEGReader
```

Lists every segment of a JPEG file and reads the tags in its EXIF segment.

Read-only, bounds-checked and fuzz-tested, like ``TIFFReader``. Written from ITU-T T.81
(JPEG, annex B: markers and segments), EXIF 3.0 section 4.5.4 (the `Exif\0\0` APP1 segment),
ICC.1 annex B.4 (the `ICC_PROFILE` APP2 segment), XMP part 3 section 1.1.3 (the XMP APP1
segment) and C2PA 2.1 section A.3.1 (JUMBF in APP11 segments).

Each segment becomes a block named `jpeg:<marker>` with an identifier where the marker is
shared, for example `jpeg:APP1:Exif` or `jpeg:APP2:ICC`. Entropy-coded data after a start of
scan is skipped up to the next marker; anything after the end-of-image marker is reported as
`jpeg:trailing-data`, because extra images (such as a multi-picture file) live there.

## `JPEGReader/blockName(marker:payload:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/JPEGReader.swift`

```swift
static func blockName(marker: UInt8, payload: [UInt8]) -> String
```

The block name of a segment.

- Parameters:
  - marker: The marker's second byte.
  - payload: The segment's bytes after its length.
- Returns: A name such as `jpeg:SOF` or `jpeg:APP1:Exif`.

## `JPEGReader/emptyIPTCSegment`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/JPEGReader.swift`

```swift
static let emptyIPTCSegment: [UInt8]
```

The APP13 segment ImageIO writes into every JPEG: "Photoshop 3.0", an empty IPTC record
(image resource `0x0404`, length 0) and that record's MD5 digest (resource `0x0425`,
the MD5 of no bytes). It holds no information; any other APP13 content is reported as
`jpeg:APP13`. Observed with ImageIO on macOS 27 and the iOS 27 Simulator.

```swift

```

The identifier that starts an EXIF APP1 segment.

```swift

```


internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/JPEGReader.swift`


## `JPEGReader/skipEntropyCodedData(_:from:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/JPEGReader.swift`

```swift
static func skipEntropyCodedData(_ bytes: [UInt8], from start: Int) -> Int
```

Skips the entropy-coded data after a start-of-scan segment.

Inside it, `0xFF 0x00` is a stuffed data byte and `0xFF 0xD0`–`0xD7` are restart
markers (T.81 B.1.1.5 and B.2.1); any other `0xFF xx` is the next marker.

- Parameters:
  - bytes: The file.
  - start: The first byte after the start-of-scan segment.
- Returns: The position of the next marker, or the end of the file.

## `Lens`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Lens.swift`

```swift
struct Lens
```

One physical camera on the device, described as plain data.

Lenses are discovered at run time by a ``CaptureBackend``; an interface renders them without
knowing the hardware (AGENTS.md, "Capabilities are data"). Every range is what the camera
reported for the format the app uses, so it can differ between devices and iOS versions.

  from that RAW (their size is ``OutputSize``).

  (`supportedMaxPhotoDimensions`) for its processed fallback, without the deferred-only
  24 MP size.
- Fewer than two sizes is no choice.

## `LensChangeBlocker`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/CameraModel+Lens.swift`

```swift
enum LensChangeBlocker
```

Why the lens can't be changed now (issue #96; docs/design/lens-while-busy.md).

Changing the lens restarts the camera. During a photo that would reconfigure the session
under it; during a series it would end the series part-way or mix lenses in it. An interface
disables its lens controls while ``CameraModel/lensChangeBlocker`` is not `nil`, and may draw
the cases differently: a single photo is usually over before a person would notice, so the
design draws the controls dimmed for ``takingPhoto`` only if it lasts, and for the others at
once.

## `LevelTapRule`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Level.swift`

```swift
struct LevelTapRule
```

Decides when the level gives its tap: the moment it becomes level, as the person moves the
device (docs/design/level-haptic.md, sections 2 and 4).

The rule is kept here, out of the interface, so it is tested to full coverage; an interface
only plays the tap (and says "Level" to VoiceOver) when ``update(_:at:isHeldBack:)`` answers
`true`.

- One tap each time the level goes from not level to level. None while it stays level, and
  none on leaving it. Changing between the horizon and the top-down level while level
  throughout is not a new entry.
- No tap for the first reading after the level appears, even if it is already level: the tap
  marks a change the person made, not the level being switched on.
- At least ``minimumInterval`` between taps. An entry sooner than that is dropped, never
  played late.
- No tap while taps are held back (``CameraModel/holdsBackLevelTap``, or a sheet over the
  camera). An entry while held back is dropped too, so when the cause ends with the device
  still level, nothing plays.

```swift
if rule.update(level.state, at: .now, isHeldBack: model.holdsBackLevelTap) { taps += 1 }
```

## `LevelTapRule/init()`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Level.swift`


## `LevelTapRule/minimumInterval`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Level.swift`

```swift
static let minimumInterval: Duration
```

The shortest time between two taps: a steady hand near the edge of the tolerance can

## `LevelTapRule/update(_:at:isHeldBack:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Level.swift`

```swift
mutating func update(_ state: LevelState?, at time: ContinuousClock.Instant, isHeldBack: Bool) -> Bool
```

Applies the level's state, and says whether to tap now.

- Parameters:
  - state: What the level shows, or `nil` when there are no readings (the level was
    switched off or its readings ended). `nil` resets the rule, so the next reading counts
    as the first.
  - time: The time of the reading, from a monotonic clock.
  - isHeldBack: Whether taps are held back now (a capture, a series, the camera not
    running, a sheet over the camera, or the app not active).
- Returns: `true` when the level has just become level and a tap is allowed.

## `LevelTracker`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Level.swift`

```swift
struct LevelTracker
```

Turns gravity readings into what the level shows, with a little hysteresis so the level
state and its haptic don't flicker at the edge of the tolerance.

The tolerances are tool settings, chosen to match what a person can see on screen: a bar
within 1 degree of the horizon looks level on a phone screen.

## `LightCaptureBackend`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Light.swift`

```swift
protocol LightCaptureBackend : CaptureBackend
```

A ``CaptureBackend`` that can also switch the torch of the running lens.

`NopeCapture`'s AVFoundation backend and ``SimulatedCaptureBackend`` implement it. With a
backend that doesn't, ``LightModel`` offers no torch.

## `LightCaptureBackend/setTorch(level:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Light.swift`

```swift
func setTorch(level: Double?) async -> TorchStatus
```

Turns the torch of the running lens on at a brightness level, or off.

The system may refuse a level while the device is hot; the backend then reports
``TorchStatus/unavailable`` and leaves the light off. Stopping the camera or switching
lenses turns the light off, so ``LightModel`` asks again after each start.

A request to turn the light on while no camera is attached (before the start, or while
the camera is reconfigured) is answered ``TorchStatus/pending``, never
``TorchStatus/off``: the light can't be switched yet, and ``LightModel`` applies it after
the start (issue #75).

- Parameter level: The brightness, from just above 0 to 1, or `nil` to turn the light
  off.
- Returns: The light's state afterwards.

## `LightModel`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Light.swift`

```swift
@MainActor final class LightModel
```

The flash and the torch: what the person chose, and what the light is doing (issue #38).

``CameraModel`` owns one as ``CameraModel/light`` and sends it ``CameraIntent/light(_:)``
intents. The rules:

- The torch stays on while framing, focusing and taking photos; while it is on, the flash
  is off for every photo, so the light doesn't change or blink when the shutter fires.
  Choosing Auto or On for the flash turns the torch off.
- The torch turns off when the camera stops, pauses (an interruption, heat) or switches to a
  lens without a torch (AGENTS.md, "Battery"). The brightness level is kept.
- After a restart of the same lens, such as a recovery from an error, a torch that was on
  comes back on.
- A request made before the camera is ready is kept as ``TorchStatus/pending`` and applied
  once the camera has started; the light then comes on or is reported unavailable, never
  silently turned off (issue #75).

Isolated to the main actor, like ``CameraModel``.

## `LightModel/applyTorch()`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Light.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func applyTorch() async
```

Asks the hardware for the torch at ``torchLevel``, and records the answer. Does nothing
without a backend.

The answer is never recorded as a silent "off" (issue #75):
- ``TorchStatus/pending`` (the camera is being reconfigured) keeps the request, which the
  next ``sessionDidStart()`` applies;
- ``TorchStatus/off`` in answer to "on" is recorded as ``TorchStatus/unavailable``, so an
  interface says the light didn't come on;
- if the person turned the light off, or the camera paused, while the hardware answered,
  a light that came on is turned off again.

## `LightModel/cameraDidPause()`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Light.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func cameraDidPause() async
```

Turns the torch off because the camera stopped or paused (an interruption, heat, or the app
leaving the screen). The person turns it on again when they want it.

Side effect: switches the torch off.

## `LiveActivityContent`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/LiveActivity.swift`

```swift
struct LiveActivityContent
```

What the Live Activity shows on the Lock Screen and in the Dynamic Island while a series or
its saving runs (issue #64): counts and status only, never image data, location or metadata
(docs/PRIVACY.md, "Live Activity").

``LiveActivityCoordinator`` makes one from the camera model and hands it to the app's
``LiveActivityPresenting``, which shows it with ActivityKit. The app and its widget extension
use it as the activity's content state, so it is `Codable`. An interface renders
``kind``, ``statusLine``, ``signingLine`` and ``progress``; it holds no rules of its own.

iOS shows an app's Live Activity only while the app isn't on screen, and the camera stops when
the app leaves the foreground, so what people see is mostly a paused series, frames still being
saved, or how the run ended.

## `LiveActivityCoordinator`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/LiveActivityCoordinator.swift`

```swift
@MainActor final class LiveActivityCoordinator
```

Starts, updates and ends the Live Activity that shows a series and its saving on the Lock
Screen and in the Dynamic Island (issue #64), and asks for time to finish saving when the app
leaves the foreground.

The app owns one for its lifetime, runs ``observe(_:)`` while the camera screen is shown, and
keeps ``isEnabled`` (the Settings switch, off by default), ``isInForeground``, ``isActive``
and ``isNightMode`` current. The coordinator follows the camera model; it holds no camera
rules.

- **Start:** an activity can only be started while the app is in the foreground (ActivityKit,
  `Activity.request`), so it starts when an interval series, astro run, burst or self-timer
  starts while the app is active (``isActive``), if the switch is on and the person allows
  Live Activities.
- **Updates:** local only, no push and no network. While the app is in the foreground the
  activity isn't on screen, so a change of the counts alone is sent at most every
  ``minimumUpdateInterval``; a change of phase, and the content when the app leaves the
  foreground, are sent at once. A countdown is drawn by the system from its end time, with no
  updates (AGENTS.md, "Battery": no timers that keep running).
- **End:** when the run ends, at once if the app is in the foreground, where the camera screen
  says how it went; otherwise its final content stays on the Lock Screen for
  ``endedActivityDuration``. Stop on the activity ends it at once (``stopSeries(_:)``).
- **Time to finish saving:** when the app leaves the foreground while photos are being taken or
  saved (``CameraModel/isFinishingPhotos``), the coordinator asks iOS for time to finish, and
  gives it back as soon as they are saved, whether or not the switch is on. If the time runs
  out first, the activity says saving is paused until the app is opened again.

## `LiveActivityDismissal/after(_:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/LiveActivity.swift`

```swift
case after(Date)
```

## `LiveViewBackend`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/LiveViewModel.swift`

```swift
protocol LiveViewBackend : CaptureBackend
```

A ``CaptureBackend`` that can analyze the live preview at a low rate (histogram and focus
peaking) and report the camera's values for the live readout.

`NopeCapture`'s AVFoundation backend and ``SimulatedCaptureBackend`` implement it. Both are
off until asked, so nothing is measured ahead of need (AGENTS.md, "Battery"), and neither
changes what is captured: the analysis reads reduced preview frames from a separate video
output, never the photo output.

second at most (``LiveUpdateRate``). ``LiveViewModel/observeAnalyses()`` is its only
consumer. The stream ends when the backend is released.

fake. The app implements it with Core Location.

Privacy rule (issue #43; AGENTS.md, "No network"): the location is read on the device and
written only into the person's own photos. Nothing is sent anywhere, and only "while using
the app" access is ever asked for.

## `LocationTagging`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/LocationTagging.swift`

```swift
@MainActor final class LocationTagging
```

Location tagging for photos (issue #43): asks for access when the person turns Location on,
follows the location only while the camera is running, and gives each photo the latest one.

The rules live here, so any interface only calls these methods:

- Location is off by default (``MetadataItem/location``). ``turnOn(in:)`` asks for access
  first and turns the switch on only if access is allowed.
- Location updates run only while the switch is on, access is allowed and the camera is
  running (``update(isOn:cameraIsRunning:)``). They stop otherwise, and the last location is
  forgotten, so a photo never gets a location from an earlier session.
- A photo taken before the first location arrives is saved without a location.

```swift
let tagging = LocationTagging(provider: provider)
await tagging.turnOn(in: settings)
await tagging.update(isOn: settings.metadata.isOn(.location), cameraIsRunning: true)
model.location = tagging
```

## `LumaFrame`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/FrameAnalysis.swift`

```swift
struct LumaFrame
```

A small grid sampled from one live preview frame: its brightness and its local sharpness.

The capture adapter samples it from a reduced, preview-sized video frame at a low rate,
only while the histogram or focus peaking is shown (AGENTS.md, "Battery"). Privacy rule: the
grid stays in memory for one analysis and is never stored, logged or written to a photo
(issues #47 and #51).

## `LumaFrame/edges`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/FrameAnalysis.swift`

```swift
let edges: [UInt8]
```

The local sharpness of each cell, 0 (flat) to 255: the brightness differences between the
cell's pixel and its neighbors in the full frame, row by row from the top.



  - width: The number of columns.
  - height: The number of rows.
  - luma: The brightness of each cell; `width` × `height` values.

## `LumaFrame/oriented(quarterTurns:mirrored:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/FrameAnalysis.swift`

```swift
func oriented(quarterTurns: Int, mirrored: Bool) -> LumaFrame
```

The frame turned and mirrored the way the viewfinder shows it.

Video frames arrive in the sensor's orientation; the viewfinder turns them by its
rotation angle and mirrors the front camera.

- Parameters:

    90 degrees.
  - mirrored: Whether the viewfinder mirrors the picture left to right (the front
    camera).
- Returns: The turned frame.

## `ManualCapabilities`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualCapabilities.swift`

```swift
struct ManualCapabilities
```

Which manual controls one lens supports, as plain values.

A ``CaptureBackend`` fills it from the camera's own answers (for example
`isExposureModeSupported(.custom)` and `isLockingFocusWithCustomLensPositionSupported`), so
an interface shows only what the lens can do (AGENTS.md, "Progressive enhancement"). The
numeric ranges (ISO, shutter, aperture, bracket count) are on ``Lens``.

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualCapabilities.swift`

```swift
let exposureCompensationRange: ClosedRange<Double>?
```

The exposure compensation the lens accepts, in EV (`minExposureTargetBias` to
`maxExposureTargetBias`), or `nil` when it has none.

## `ManualCapabilities/exposureModes`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualCapabilities.swift`

```swift
var exposureModes: [ExposureMode] { get }
```

The exposure modes the lens supports, in the order P, S, I, M. Program is always
offered: it is the camera's own auto exposure.

## `ManualCapabilities/init(supportsManualExposure:supportsShutterPriority:supportsISOPriority:supportsAperturePriority:exposureCompensationRange:supportsExposureLock:supportsAutoFocus:supportsFocusLock:supportsManualFocus:supportsManualWhiteBalance:meteringModes:supportsFocusPoint:supportsExposurePoint:supportsNearFocusLimit:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualCapabilities.swift`

```swift
init(supportsManualExposure: Bool, supportsShutterPriority: Bool, supportsISOPriority: Bool, supportsAperturePriority: Bool, exposureCompensationRange: ClosedRange<Double>?, supportsExposureLock: Bool, supportsAutoFocus: Bool, supportsFocusLock: Bool, supportsManualFocus: Bool, supportsManualWhiteBalance: Bool, meteringModes: [MeteringMode], supportsFocusPoint: Bool = false, supportsExposurePoint: Bool = false, supportsNearFocusLimit: Bool = false)
```

Creates a capability description.

- Parameters:
  - supportsManualExposure: Whether ISO and shutter can both be set.
  - supportsShutterPriority: Whether shutter priority is supported.
  - supportsISOPriority: Whether ISO priority is supported.
  - supportsAperturePriority: Whether aperture priority is supported.
  - exposureCompensationRange: The exposure compensation range in EV, or `nil`.
  - supportsExposureLock: Whether auto exposure can be locked.
  - supportsAutoFocus: Whether the lens can autofocus.
  - supportsFocusLock: Whether autofocus can be locked.
  - supportsManualFocus: Whether the focus can be set to a lens position.
  - supportsManualWhiteBalance: Whether the white balance can be set.
  - meteringModes: The metering modes offered.
  - supportsFocusPoint: Whether autofocus can aim at a chosen point. Defaults to `false`.
  - supportsExposurePoint: Whether auto exposure can meter a chosen point. Defaults to
    `false`.
  - supportsNearFocusLimit: Whether autofocus can be limited to near subjects. Defaults
    to `false`.

## `ManualCaptureBackend`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualCaptureBackend.swift`

```swift
protocol ManualCaptureBackend : CaptureBackend
```

A ``CaptureBackend`` that can also set the camera's exposure, focus and white balance, report
what the camera is using, and take exposure brackets.

`NopeCapture`'s AVFoundation backend and ``SimulatedCaptureBackend`` implement it. A backend
that doesn't simply has no manual controls: ``ManualControlsModel`` then offers none.

## `ManualCaptureBackend/applyControls(_:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualCaptureBackend.swift`

```swift
func applyControls(_ controls: DeviceControls) async throws(CaptureError)
```

Sets exposure, focus, white balance and metering on the running camera.

Starting the camera or switching lenses returns every control to automatic, so
``ManualControlsModel`` applies its controls again after each start.

- Parameter controls: The controls, already checked against the lens.
- Throws: ``CaptureError/notRunning`` if the camera isn't running, or
  ``CaptureError/configurationFailed`` if the camera refused the change.

## `ManualCaptureBackend/captureBracket(_:frames:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualCaptureBackend.swift`

```swift
func captureBracket(_ request: CaptureRequest, frames: [BracketFrame]) async throws(CaptureError) -> [CapturedPhoto]
```

Takes an exposure bracket: one separate single-frame photo per frame, nothing merged.

- Parameters:
  - request: The lens and format; the same as the last ``CaptureBackend/startSession(_:)``.
  - frames: Each frame's exact ISO and exposure duration, no more than the lens's
    ``Lens/maxBracketedPhotoCount``.
- Returns: The photos, in the order of `frames`.
- Throws: ``CaptureError/notRunning`` if the camera isn't running,
  ``CaptureError/blankPicture`` if any frame is blank (every frame is dropped), or
  ``CaptureError/captureFailed`` if the system reports an error or too few photos.

## `ManualCaptureBackend/exposureReadings`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualCaptureBackend.swift`

```swift
var exposureReadings: AsyncStream<ExposureReading> { get }
```

What the camera is using now, sent while readings are on
(``setExposureReadingsEnabled(_:)``), at a reduced rate. ``ManualControlsModel/observeReadings()``
is its only consumer. The stream ends when the backend is released.

## `ManualCaptureBackend/setExposureReadingsEnabled(_:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualCaptureBackend.swift`

```swift
func setExposureReadingsEnabled(_ enabled: Bool) async
```

Turns the readings on or off. They are off by default. ``ManualControlsModel`` turns them
on once the camera runs, because full auto's fast shutter rule and the close-up hint read
them (issue #56); they arrive at a reduced rate and stop with the camera, so nothing is
measured while the camera screen isn't in use (AGENTS.md, "Battery").

- Parameter enabled: Whether to send readings.

## `ManualCaptureBackend/setFocus(lensPosition:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualCaptureBackend.swift`

```swift
func setFocus(lensPosition: Double) async throws(CaptureError) -> Double
```

A backend whose focus can't be locked at a position: every request fails with
``CaptureError/configurationFailed``, so no focus bracket frame is taken.

- Parameter lensPosition: The position asked for.
- Returns: Never returns.
- Throws: ``CaptureError/configurationFailed``, always.

## `ManualControl`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControl.swift`

```swift
enum ManualControl
```

One of the six manual controls, in the fixed order every interface shows them.

The order is the same on every device (the manual controls design: "Six slots in the same
order on every device"). A control the selected lens can't set stays in its place, and
``ManualControlsModel/availability(of:)`` says why.

## `ManualControlsModel`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControlsModel.swift`

```swift
@MainActor final class ManualControlsModel
```

The manual and pro controls: the state behind the control grid, the exposure modes, the
locks, bracketing and interval shooting.

``CameraModel`` owns one as ``CameraModel/manual``. An interface reads its properties and
sends ``ManualControlIntent`` values through ``CameraModel/send(_:)`` as
``CameraIntent/manual(_:)``. Every rule lives here: which controls a lens offers
(``availability(of:)``), the steps of each control (``scale(for:)``), and how the exposure
modes combine. Full auto is the default, and manual controls stay hidden until the "Manual
controls" option turns them on (``isEnabled``).

## `ManualControlsModel/apply(restoringOnFailure:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControlsModel.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func apply(restoringOnFailure previous: ManualSettings) async
```

Sends the settings to the camera. If the camera refuses them, the settings go back to
what they were; if it isn't running, they are applied when it starts.

- Parameter previous: The settings to restore when the camera refuses.

## `ManualControlsModel/applyCurrentControls()`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControlsModel+Auto.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func applyCurrentControls() async
```

Sends the settings and aids to the camera. If it refuses, nothing is kept from the
change except what the interface shows; if it isn't running, the controls are applied
when it starts.

## `ManualControlsModel/assist`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControlsModel+Auto.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var assist: AssistSettings { get }
```

The aids to send with the person's settings. The fast shutter limit is full auto's alone:
with manual controls on, the person decides.

## `ManualControlsModel/autoFocusIsInUse`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControlsModel+Auto.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var autoFocusIsInUse: Bool { get }
```

Whether the selected lens is autofocusing now: it can autofocus, and focus is neither set
by hand nor locked in Manual Controls. The same rule ``DeviceControls/init(settings:lens:assist:)``
follows, so it is `false` exactly when the camera gets no Close-Up or Far Focus limit.

Close-Up and Far Focus (``nearFocusLimit``, ``farFocusLimit``) limit only autofocus
(`AVCaptureDevice.autoFocusRangeRestriction`), so an interface shows them only while this
is `true` (issue #91, docs/design/SETTING-SCOPE.md, 6). The person's choice is kept and is
used again when focus returns to Auto. `false` before the camera is ready.

```swift
@MainActor func availability(of control: ManualControl) -> ControlAvailability
```

Whether a control can be used on the selected lens now, and if not, why.

- Parameter control: A control.
- Returns: The control's availability.


The exposure bracket for the next press of the shutter, or `nil` when bracketing is off.
It works in full auto and with manual controls on (docs/design/BRACKETING.md, decision
2). An interface shows it as the drive strip's Bracket word.


Whether the person set the shutter but not ISO, so a bracket changes ISO first; an
interface says which value changes from frame to frame.

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControlsModel.swift`

```swift

```

The bracket frame counts the selected lens offers; empty when it can't bracket.

## `ManualControlsModel/bracketFrames(once:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControlsModel.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func bracketFrames(once bracket: BracketSettings) -> [BracketFrame]?
```

The frames of a bracket taken once, for a Siri or Shortcuts request (issue #62), whether
or not manual controls are on. It leaves ``bracket`` and every setting as they are.

The base exposure is the person's ISO and shutter where set (only while manual controls
are on; otherwise the settings are automatic), otherwise the camera's last reading.

- Parameter bracket: The bracket.
- Returns: The frames, or `nil` when the lens can't take it (``canBracket(_:)``) or the
  camera hasn't reported an exposure yet.

## `ManualControlsModel/bracketPlan`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControlsModel.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var bracketPlan: [BracketFramePlan]? { get }
```

The frames the next press of the shutter takes, as an interface lists them in Bracket
Settings, or `nil` for a single photo: bracketing is off, the lens reports no ISO or
shutter range, or there is nothing to plan from yet (see ``bracketFrames(once:)``). The
bracket always fits the lens: ``lensWillStart(_:)`` drops one that doesn't. It follows the camera's readings, so it changes as the light
does; the bracket is planned again from the reading when the shutter is pressed.

## `ManualControlsModel/bracketPlan(once:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControlsModel.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func bracketPlan(once bracket: BracketSettings) -> [BracketFramePlan]?
```

Plans a bracket's frames around the base exposure, within the lens's ranges: the
person's ISO and shutter where set (only while manual controls are on), otherwise the
camera's last reading, which already includes exposure compensation. The parameter the
person didn't set changes first, the shutter when neither is set.

- Parameter bracket: The bracket.
- Returns: One plan per frame, in the order taken, or `nil` when the lens can't take the
  bracket or there is no base exposure yet.

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControlsModel.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var followsFaces: Bool { get set }
```

Whether faces the camera detects steer autofocus and auto exposure. On by default, the
system's behavior; the app sets it from the person's Face Detection setting
(``AutoControlIntent/setFollowsFaces(_:)``). All Auto doesn't change it.

## `ManualControlsModel/handle(_:capture:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControlsModel.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func handle(_ intent: ManualControlIntent, capture: @escaping @MainActor () async -> Bool) async
```

Handles one intent. ``CameraModel/send(_:)`` calls it.

Settings intents have no effect while manual controls are off. Interval shooting and
bracketing work in full auto too (each photo is then a full-auto photo, and a bracket is
planned around the camera's reading), so they always apply.

- Parameters:
  - intent: The intent.
  - capture: Takes one photo of an interval series the way the shutter does, waiting
    while the series is paused, and returns whether the photo was taken and saved.

## `ManualControlsModel/holdsControls`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControlsModel.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var holdsControls: Bool { get set }
```

Whether the camera's controls are held by a focus bracket, which sets focus, exposure and
white balance itself while it runs or while an end of its range is set. Changes to the
settings are kept, and applied when the hold ends.

## `ManualControlsModel/observeReadings()`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControlsModel.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func observeReadings() async
```

Applies the backend's readings until its stream ends. Interfaces
run it in a `.task` while the camera is visible. Returns at once without manual controls.

``reading`` shows a reading only while manual controls are on; in full auto the readings
drive the fast shutter rule and the hints (``record(_:)``).

## `ManualControlsModel/resetAidsForNewLens()`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControlsModel+Auto.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func resetAidsForNewLens()
```

Clears what belonged to the previous lens: the tapped point, the close-up state and the
hint, and starts the new lens at its fastest fast-shutter limit until it reports a
reading.

## `ManualControlsModel/resetReadingAids()`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControlsModel+Auto.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func resetReadingAids()
```

Starts the fast shutter rule and the steady hint again from the lens's fastest limit,
forgetting the last reading: after a lens change, and when manual controls turn on or off
(Astro too), the readings taken before belong to another exposure. Without this, a limit
lifted before Manual stayed lifted after it, and the hint stayed until enough new
readings brought the limit back past the half-stop ``AutoShutter/returnMargin``.

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControlsModel.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var showsSteadyHint: Bool { get set }
```

Whether the camera should say "Hold it steady or use a tripod": in full auto when the
light is too low for a handheld-safe shutter, and with manual controls when the shutter
chosen or in use is slower than that. It follows only readings taken in the current mode
and lens. Interfaces show the hint from ``CameraModel/showsSteadyHint``, which leaves it
to the astro helper's own guidance while that is on.

```swift
struct ManualSettings
```

The manual settings the person chose. A `nil` value means Auto: the camera sets it.

``automatic`` is full auto, the default. ``ManualControlsModel`` changes the settings in
response to intents and keeps them consistent with the selected lens; interfaces only read
them.

## `MetadataAllowlist`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataAllowlist.swift`

```swift
struct MetadataAllowlist
```

Everything one file may carry: the exact tags, container blocks, XMP properties and ImageIO
keys, for one format and one set of switches.

``MetadataPlan/allowlist(for:signed:)`` builds it; ``MetadataVerifier`` checks a read-back
file against it. Anything not listed fails the check (AGENTS.md, "Metadata is allowlisted
and verified, never blocklisted").

## `MetadataAllowlist/TextRule/any`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataAllowlist.swift`

```swift
case any
```

Any text: the value is the system's own (for example the system version).

## `MetadataAllowlist/TextRule/blankDate`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataAllowlist.swift`

```swift
case blankDate
```

A date with no date in it: empty, or at most ``blankDateMaximumLength`` characters
that are each a blank or a colon. This is the Exif standard's form for an unknown date
("all the character spaces except colons filled with blank characters", Exif 2.32 and
3.0, `DateTime`) however the system pads or shortens it; the reader stops at the first
NUL, so NUL padding passes too. Any digit or other character fails.

## `MetadataAllowlist/TextRule/blankDateMaximumLength`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataAllowlist.swift`

```swift
static let blankDateMaximumLength: Int
```

The length of an Exif date and time, `YYYY:MM:DD HH:MM:SS`, without its closing NUL
(Exif 2.32, `DateTime`: 20 bytes with it).

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataAllowlist.swift`

```swift
case exactly(String)
```

Exactly this text.


Exactly one of these texts.

## `MetadataAllowlist/init(format:tags:wildcardDirectories:blocks:xmpProperties:imageIOKeys:wildcardImageIODictionaries:requiredTags:textRules:xmpTextRules:allowsSubSecondDates:allowsTimeZoneDates:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataAllowlist.swift`

```swift
init(format: PhotoFormat, tags: Set<TagID>, wildcardDirectories: Set<TIFFDirectory>, blocks: Set<String>, xmpProperties: Set<XMPName>, imageIOKeys: Set<ImageIOKey>, wildcardImageIODictionaries: Set<String>, requiredTags: Set<TagID>, textRules: [TagID : TextRule], xmpTextRules: [XMPName : TextRule], allowsSubSecondDates: Bool, allowsTimeZoneDates: Bool)
```

Creates an allowlist. ``MetadataPlan`` is the only producer in the app.

- Parameters:
  - format: The format.
  - tags: The allowed tags.
  - wildcardDirectories: Directories allowed as a whole.

  - allowsTimeZoneDates: Whether XMP dates may have a time zone.

## `MetadataComparison`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataComparison.swift`

```swift
struct MetadataComparison
```

The metadata of the app's saved file and of the phone's fully processed version, side by side
(issue #55, "Compare the metadata").

Both sides come from the project's own tag reader (``MetadataFileReader``): the app's from the
read-back of the saved file, the phone's from the in-memory file iOS wrote. Each tag, block
and XMP property becomes one ``Entry``, grouped by ``Difference``.

Privacy: built and shown in memory only; nothing is stored, logged or copied. Values that
can identify the device or link photos are marked ``Entry/isIdentifying`` so an interface
masks them until the person asks to see them.

## `MetadataFileReader`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataFileReader.swift`

```swift
enum MetadataFileReader
```

The project's own tag reader: reads a saved file's bytes back and lists everything
metadata-like in it (AGENTS.md, "Fail closed").

It dispatches on the file's format: the TIFF structure of a DNG (``TIFFReader``), the
segments of a JPEG (``JPEGReader``) or the boxes and items of a HEIF (``HEIFReader``). Each
reader lists every tag, block and property it meets, known or not, so a tag a future iOS adds
is reported and rejected rather than missed. ``MetadataVerifier`` then compares the result
with the allowlist.

## `MetadataInventory`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataInventory.swift`

```swift
struct MetadataInventory
```

Everything metadata-like that the read-back found in one file: every TIFF tag in every
directory, every container block (JPEG segment, HEIF box, item and property), and every
XMP property.

``MetadataFileReader`` builds it from the file's bytes; ``MetadataVerifier`` compares it with
the allowlist. Privacy: it holds the file's tag values only in memory, and a ``SavedPhoto``
keeps it for the "What's in your last photo" screen (issue #1); it is never logged.

## `MetadataItem`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataSettings.swift`

```swift
enum MetadataItem
```

One optional metadata item a person can switch on or off.

Each case is a row of the "Switches" table in `docs/PRIVACY.md` ("Metadata plan"). Removing
any of them never breaks the photo. Items the photo can't work without are locked and have no
case here (``MetadataField/Group/locked(_:)``). The Content Credentials signature has its own
switch outside this list (``MetadataSettings/signsPhotos``).

## `MetadataPlan`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataPlan.swift`

```swift
struct MetadataPlan
```

Turns the person's ``MetadataSettings`` into each file's metadata: the allowlist a file is
checked against, and the ImageIO property dictionary a file is written with.

Privacy rule: a file's metadata is built from the allowlist, never by deleting known tags
from what the system wrote (AGENTS.md, "Metadata is allowlisted and verified"). The builder
starts from an empty dictionary and copies in only the values of fields in
``MetadataField/catalog`` that the switches allow, so a tag a future iOS adds is never copied.

## `MetadataPlan/addStandardFileTags(for:to:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataPlan.swift`

```swift
func addStandardFileTags(for format: PhotoFormat, to result: inout [String : Any])
```

Adds the standard file tags (``MetadataSettings/writesStandardFileTags``) a file doesn't
have yet: in a HEIF or JPEG the EXIF `ExifVersion`, `ComponentsConfiguration`,
`FlashpixVersion` and `ColorSpace`, and in any file with a GPS directory its `GPSVersionID`. Each is a fixed
value from the Exif standard on the allowlist (locked image structure, or the location
item's GPS fields), so nothing identifying is added; a value the source already has is
kept.

ImageIO writes no `YCbCrPositioning` (IFD0 `0x0213`): `CGImageProperties.h` has no key for
it, and neither the `{TIFF}` dictionary nor an XMP `tiff:YCbCrPositioning` tag puts it in
a JPEG's EXIF (macOS 27.2, 2026-11-16). It also drops `ColorSpace` from a Display P3
JPEG; a Display P3 HEIF keeps it (`docs/TESTING.md`, "External validators").

- Parameters:
  - format: The file's format.
  - result: The property dictionary being built.

## `MetadataPlan/contentCredentialsTag`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataPlan.swift`

```swift
static let contentCredentialsTag: MetadataAllowlist.TagID
```

Where a Content Credentials manifest lives in each format, allowed only in a signed file.
From the C2PA 2.1 specification, appendix A ("Embedding manifests"): JUMBF in JPEG APP11
segments (A.3.1), a top-level `uuid` box in ISOBMFF files such as HEIF (A.4), and TIFF
tag 52545 (`0xCD41`) for TIFF and DNG (A.3.3). The signing module confirms these when it
lands.

## `MetadataPlan/isWritten(_:in:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataPlan.swift`

```swift
static func isWritten(_ key: ImageIOKey, in format: PhotoFormat) -> Bool
```

Whether a value under an ImageIO key is copied into a file of a format.

A HEIF or JPEG never gets the `{ExifAux}` dictionary (issue #58). ImageIO reports a DNG's
`LensSpecification` and `LensModel` again as `{ExifAux}` `LensInfo` and `LensModel`, so
a developed copy's source has both; written back, ImageIO stores the `{ExifAux}` values
as XMP `aux:LensInfo` and `aux:Lens` properties, which no allowlist permits, and the
read-back rejected the photo: with "Lens make and model" on and a HEIF or JPEG selected,
nothing was saved (`FileCompatibilityTests`, iOS 27.2 Simulator, 2026-11-14). The same
values are still written as the EXIF `LensSpecification` and `LensModel` tags. A DNG keeps
every key: its metadata is written by AVFoundation, which the device tag check covers.

- Parameters:
  - key: The ImageIO key.
  - format: The file's format.
- Returns: `false` for an `{ExifAux}` key in a HEIF or JPEG; otherwise `true`.

## `MetadataPlan/location`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataPlan.swift`

```swift
var location: PhotoLocation?
```

Where the photo was taken, written when ``MetadataItem/location`` is on, or `nil` for no
location (issue #43). ``CameraModel`` sets it for each capture from ``LocationTagging``;
nothing else supplies a location, and the system's own metadata carries none.

## `MetadataPlan/mirroredOrientation(_:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataPlan.swift`

```swift
static func mirroredOrientation(_ orientation: Int) -> Int
```

The orientation that shows a photo mirrored left to right, for an EXIF/TIFF orientation
value (issue #45).

TIFF 6.0 (`Orientation`, tag 274) and EXIF 3.0 define eight values: 1 upright, 2 mirrored,
3 turned 180°, 4 turned 180° and mirrored, 5 mirrored and turned 90° counterclockwise
(a transpose), 6 turned 90° clockwise, 7 mirrored and turned 90° clockwise (a transverse)
and 8 turned 90° counterclockwise. Mirroring the upright result left to right swaps each
pair: 1↔2, 3↔4, 5↔6 and 7↔8. The sensor data is never changed.

- Parameter orientation: The orientation the photo would be shown with, 1 to 8. Any other
  value is treated as 1, upright.
- Returns: The mirrored orientation, 1 to 8.

## `MetadataPlan/mirrorsHorizontally`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataPlan.swift`

```swift
var mirrorsHorizontally: Bool
```

Whether the photo is saved mirrored left to right: a front camera photo with
``SaveSettings/mirrorsFrontCamera`` on (issue #45). The mirroring is done only through
the orientation value (``mirroredOrientation(_:)``): the sensor data is never changed.
``CameraModel`` sets it for each capture. Off by default.


The Photoshop namespace, used by ImageIO for `DateCreated` (XMP part 2, 3.2).

## `MetadataPlan/properties(for:from:orientation:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataPlan.swift`

```swift
func properties(for format: PhotoFormat, from source: [String : Any], orientation: Int? = nil) -> [String : Any]
```

Builds the ImageIO property dictionary to write a file with, from the system's metadata.

Privacy rule: the result starts empty, and only allowlisted values are copied in
(AGENTS.md, "Build metadata from the allowlist"). The app's name, the generic camera model,
the person's name and copyright, the ``location`` and a mirrored orientation
(``mirrorsHorizontally``) are written by the app; everything else is the system's own value. A DNG's dictionary always has the ``requiredRAWTIFFFields``, with an empty
string for each one whose switch is off.

- Parameters:
  - format: The file's format.
  - source: The system's metadata, as ImageIO property dictionaries (for example
    `AVCapturePhoto.metadata`, or the properties of the DNG a HEIF is developed from).
  - orientation: An orientation to write instead of the source's, such as 1 (up) for a
    photo developed upright; `nil` keeps the source's.
- Returns: The dictionary, keyed like `CGImageProperties.h`.

## `MetadataPlan/requiredDNGTags`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataPlan.swift`

```swift
static let requiredDNGTags: Set<MetadataAllowlist.TagID>
```

The tags a DNG can't be opened without (DNG 1.7, chapter 4): `CFARepeatPatternDim`,
`CFAPattern`, `DNGVersion`, `UniqueCameraModel` and `ColorMatrix1`.

## `MetadataPlan/requiredRAWTIFFFields`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataPlan.swift`

```swift
static let requiredRAWTIFFFields: [MetadataField]
```

The `{TIFF}` fields a DNG's replacement metadata must always contain: `DateTime`, `Make`,
`Model` and `Software`.

Measured on an iPhone 16 Pro Max with iOS 27.2.1 (issue #65):
`AVCapturePhoto.fileDataRepresentation(with:)` returns `nil` for a Bayer RAW photo unless
the replacement metadata's `{TIFF}` dictionary has all four keys. Leaving out any one of
them fails; an empty string for each works; `NSNull` doesn't. Apple doesn't document the
requirement. Privacy rule: when a field's switch is off, the app writes an empty string, never
a made-up value, so the DNG still says nothing about the date, camera or software; with the
standard file tags on, the date is the Exif standard's all-blank "unknown" form instead
(``unknownDateTime``), which holds no date either.

## `MetadataPlan/systemVersion(fromSoftware:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataPlan.swift`

```swift
static func systemVersion(fromSoftware software: String?) -> String?
```

The system's version in a `Software` value: the value itself when the system wrote it, the
part in parentheses when the app wrote both ("Photivra Nope Camera 0.2.0 (27.2.1)"), or none.

A HEIF or JPEG is developed from the DNG's metadata, whose `Software` the app already
wrote, so reading it as the system's own value would nest the app's name:
"Photivra Nope Camera 0.2.0 (Photivra Nope Camera 0.2.0 (27.2.1))".

- Parameter software: The source's `Software` value, if any.
- Returns: The system's version, or `nil` if there is none (also for an empty value).

## `MetadataPlan/unknownDateTime`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataPlan.swift`

```swift
static let unknownDateTime: String
```

The `DateTime` a DNG carries when its date switch is off and the standard file tags are
on: the Exif standard's form for an unknown date, "all the character spaces except colons
filled with blank characters" (Exif 2.32 and 3.0, `DateTime`). It says nothing about when
the photo was taken, and `exiftool -validate` accepts it, where it reports an empty string
as an invalid date. It is still a value, so AVFoundation's need for the key (issue #65)
is met.

## `MetadataSettings`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataSettings.swift`

```swift
struct MetadataSettings
```

The person's metadata choices: the master switch, one switch per optional item, and the
signature switch.

This is the settings model the Settings screens are built on, and the only input the
metadata allowlist takes (``MetadataPlan``). It holds the rules of `docs/PRIVACY.md`,
"Metadata plan", so an interface only shows the values and calls the mutating methods:

- "Remove all optional details" (``removesAllOptionalDetails``) is on by default, and then
  every item is off.
- Turning any item on turns the master off; the interface then shows "Custom" (``isCustom``).
- Turning the master back on turns every item off again.
- Turning the master off sets every item to its documented default
  (``MetadataItem/isOnWhenCustomizing``).
- Signing (``signsPhotos``) is outside the master switch and off by default (issue #80).

```swift
var settings = MetadataSettings()          // master on, every item off
settings.set(.location, isOn: true)        // master off ("Custom"), location on
settings.setRemovesAllOptionalDetails(true) // every item off again
```

The type is `Codable`, so an interface can store it; nothing in `NopeCore` writes it to disk.

## `MetadataSettings/signsPhotos`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataSettings.swift`

```swift
var signsPhotos: Bool
```

Whether each photo is signed with Content Credentials. Outside the master switch; off by
default (owner decision, issue #80): a signed photo shows that it was taken with the app
and which version (the manifest's claim generator, `docs/PRIVACY.md`, "Content
Credentials"), so signing is the person's choice. A stored value is kept as saved.

## `MetadataSettings/writesStandardFileTags`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataSettings.swift`

```swift
var writesStandardFileTags: Bool { get set }
```

Whether files carry the tags the Exif standard requires that say nothing about the photo,
the device or the person ("Standard File Tags", owner decision 2026-11-16): on by default,
and outside the master switch, like ``signsPhotos``.

When on, ``MetadataPlan`` writes into HEIF and JPEG files the EXIF `ExifVersion` (2.32),
`ComponentsConfiguration` (Y, Cb, Cr), `FlashpixVersion` (1.0) and `ColorSpace` (sRGB, or
uncalibrated for Display P3), into every file with a location the GPS `GPSVersionID`
(2.3.0.0), and into a DNG whose date switch is off a `DateTime` of blanks and colons,
the Exif standard's "unknown" form, instead of an empty string. Off writes the minimal set
the app wrote before. None of the values identifies anything.

## `MetadataVerifier`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataVerifier.swift`

```swift
enum MetadataVerifier
```

Compares a read-back file with its allowlist (AGENTS.md, "Fail closed").

A file passes only with no violation at all. The save pipeline (``PhotoSaver``) never adds a
file that fails to Photos.

## `MetadataVerifier/accepts(_:name:allowlist:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataVerifier.swift`

```swift
static func accepts(_ property: MetadataInventory.XMPProperty, name: MetadataAllowlist.XMPName, allowlist: MetadataAllowlist) -> Bool
```

Whether an allowed XMP property's value is allowed: a property that mirrors a tag follows
that tag's rule, and a date carries a fraction of a second or a time zone only when those
switches are on.

- Parameters:
  - property: The property.
  - name: Its name.
  - allowlist: The allowlist.
- Returns: `true` if the value is allowed.

## `MetadataVerifier/violations(in:allowlist:expectedRawImageData:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/MetadataVerifier.swift`

```swift
static func violations(in readBack: MetadataReadBack, allowlist: MetadataAllowlist, expectedRawImageData: Data?) -> [MetadataViolation]
```

Lists every way a file breaks its allowlist.

- Parameters:
  - readBack: What the read-back found in the file.
  - allowlist: What the file may carry.
  - expectedRawImageData: For a DNG, the RAW image data the camera returned; ignored for
    other formats.
- Returns: The violations, in file order; empty means the file passes.

## `MeteringMode`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/ManualControl.swift`

```swift
enum MeteringMode
```

How the camera measures the scene for auto exposure.

AVFoundation offers no named metering patterns. The app offers what the camera's exposure
region of interest can express (`AVCaptureDevice.exposureRectOfInterest`, iOS 26 and
later), and names each by the region it measures, not by a pattern it can't promise.


The camera's default: a region at the center of the frame.


A small region at the center: the smallest region the lens supports.

```swift
case wholeFrame
```

The whole frame.

## `NopeSettings`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/NopeSettings.swift`

```swift
@MainActor final class NopeSettings
```

The person's settings, stored on the device: what each photo saves (``SaveSettings``: formats,
metadata and signing) and the camera screen options.

Every interface reads the same object and changes it only through its methods, which apply
the rules of ``SaveSettings`` and ``MetadataSettings`` (at least one format, the metadata
master switch). Each change is saved at once. The app passes ``save`` to
``CameraModel/saveSettings`` whenever it changes, so the next photo uses it.

Privacy rule (AGENTS.md, "No network, no analytics"): settings are stored in `UserDefaults` on
the device only: the app's App Group store, shared with its extensions (``SharedSettingsStore``).
They are never synced through iCloud or sent anywhere.

The model is isolated to the main actor, so an interface can read its properties directly.

```swift
let settings = NopeSettings(defaults: .standard)
settings.setFormat(.jpeg, isSelected: true)
settings.setSignsPhotos(false)
model.saveSettings = settings.save
```

## `NopeSettings/apply(_:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/NopeSettingsSnapshot.swift`

```swift
@discardableResult @MainActor func apply(_ snapshot: NopeSettingsSnapshot) -> Bool
```

Replaces the stored settings with a snapshot's, and reloads them.

A setting missing from the snapshot goes back to its default. Every value is read back
through the same rules as a stored one, so a value this version doesn't know falls back to
the default (AGENTS.md, "Fail closed"). A snapshot that can't be read changes nothing.

Night mode then is the snapshot's, a choice the person made by applying it (a preset), so
the astro helper no longer turns it off when it ends (``isNightModeFromAstro``).

- Parameter snapshot: The snapshot, from ``makeSnapshot()``.
- Returns: `true` when the snapshot could be read.

## `NopeSettings/canTurnOff(_:deviceSupportsBayerRAW:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/NopeSettings.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func canTurnOff(_ format: PhotoFormat, deviceSupportsBayerRAW: Bool) -> Bool
```

Whether the person can turn a selected format off.

Every photo needs at least one format the device can save, so the last one can't be
turned off.

- Parameters:
  - format: A selected format.
  - deviceSupportsBayerRAW: Whether any camera on the device gives Bayer RAW.
- Returns: `false` when the format is the only one left that the device can save.

## `NopeSettings/isNightModeFromAstro`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/NopeSettings.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var isNightModeFromAstro: Bool { get set }
```

Whether night mode is on because the astro helper turned it on, and the person hasn't
changed it since. Leaving the helper turns night mode off again while this is `true`
(unless ``keepsNightModeAfterAstro``).

It is stored in the shared store, so a change from another process (a Control Center
control) clears it; a relaunch clears it too, keeping night mode as it was
(``CameraModel/forgetNightModeFromAstroAtLaunch(settings:)``). It is
kept under a key outside ``SharedSettingsStore/keyPrefix``: it is the app's state, not a
setting, so presets and the Lock Screen camera's snapshot don't carry it.

## `NopeSettings/isNightModeOn`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/NopeSettings.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var isNightModeOn: Bool { get set }
```

Whether night mode is on: every screen of the app in red on black, to keep eyes used to the
dark (issue #61). Off by default, remembered between launches, never switched on or off
automatically. The app never changes the screen's brightness; people use the device's own
brightness control.

Other features read it to work in the dark. The astro helper turns it on, and turns it off
again when the helper ends if it turned it on (``CameraModel/setAstro(_:settings:)``).

## `NopeSettings/keepsNightModeAfterAstro`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/NopeSettings.swift`

```swift
@MainActor var keepsNightModeAfterAstro: Bool { get set }
```

Whether night mode stays on after the astro helper ends, when the helper turned it on.
Off by default: leaving Astro returns night mode to what it was before (owner decision for
1.0.0). A night mode the person turned on, or changed while the helper was on, is never
turned off by the helper either way.

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/NopeSettings.swift`

```swift

```

Turns night mode on or off as the person's choice, and saves the change. Use it for every
control the person uses: the Night Mode switch, the Control Center and Lock Screen control,
and Siri and Shortcuts.

## `NopeSettings/setNightModeFromAstro(_:)`

## `NopeSettingsSnapshot`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/NopeSettingsSnapshot.swift`

```swift
struct NopeSettingsSnapshot
```

A copy of every stored ``NopeSettings`` value, for a process that can't read the shared store:
the Lock Screen camera (issue #42).

Apple's LockedCameraCapture documentation says a capture extension "can't read from or write to
the App Group's shared container", and offers the `appContext` of its `CameraCaptureIntent`
instead: "up to four KB" that the app writes and the extension reads when it opens. The app
writes a snapshot there whenever the settings change; the Lock Screen camera applies it to its
own settings with ``NopeSettings/apply(_:)``, so it saves photos in the person's formats, with
their metadata and signing choices and their night mode.

The snapshot holds settings only: Booleans, names of choices and numbers. It holds no photo,
location or other personal data, and stays on the device.

It copies every key ``SharedSettingsStore/isSettingsKey(_:)`` accepts, so a setting added later
is included without changing this type.

## `NopeSettingsSnapshot/appContextLimit`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/NopeSettingsSnapshot.swift`

```swift
static let appContextLimit: Int
```

The most bytes Apple allows in a `CameraCaptureIntent`'s `appContext` (LockedCameraCapture,
"Creating a camera experience for the Lock Screen": "up to four KB").

## `NopeSettingsSnapshot/encodedSize()`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/NopeSettingsSnapshot.swift`

```swift
func encodedSize() throws -> Int
```

The size of the snapshot once encoded as JSON, the larger of the two encodings an
`appContext` may use, in bytes. Compare it with ``appContextLimit``.

- Returns: The size in bytes.
- Throws: An `EncodingError` if the snapshot can't be encoded.

```swift
case sRGB
```

sRGB, for sites and screens that show only sRGB. Colors outside sRGB are clipped to it.

## `OutputSize`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/DevelopedFileOptions.swift`

```swift
enum OutputSize
```

The size of the developed copies, as a fraction of the RAW's width and height (issue #57,
"Downscaled size").

A smaller copy is for sharing or saving space; the RAW always keeps every pixel. The encoder
draws the developed image into a smaller bitmap with Core Graphics' high-quality
interpolation, so the copy's exact size is known and its EXIF `PixelXDimension` and
`PixelYDimension` match it. A fraction, rather than a pixel count, keeps the choice the same
on every lens and never enlarges a photo.

## `OutputSize/dimensions(of:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/DevelopedFileOptions.swift`

```swift
func dimensions(of size: PhotoDimensions) -> PhotoDimensions
```

The size of a copy of an image.

- Parameter size: The developed image's size, in pixels.
- Returns: Each side times ``scale``, rounded, and at least 1 pixel.

```swift
case full
```

The RAW's own width and height. The default.

```swift
case half
```

Half the width and height: a quarter of the pixels (about 3 MP from a 12 MP RAW).

```swift
case quarter
```

## `OverlayOptions`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/OverlayOptions.swift`

```swift
struct OverlayOptions
```

The viewfinder overlays the person turned on: guides (grid, level) and live aids (focus
peaking, histogram, live camera readout). Every overlay is off by default (issues #40, #47,
#51 and #52).

Privacy rule (AGENTS.md, "No network, no analytics"): overlays are on screen only. Nothing
they show is written to the photo, stored or logged.

## `PhoneLink`

```swift
@MainActor protocol PhoneLink : AnyObject
```

The Apple Watch side's link to the iPhone app: sends a message and waits for the reply.

The watch app implements it with WatchConnectivity (`WCSession.sendMessageData`); tests and
the watch UI tests pass a fake. Only the bytes of ``WatchCommand`` values cross it.

## `PhoneProcessedPhoto`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/Comparison.swift`

```swift
struct PhoneProcessedPhoto
```

The phone's fully processed version of (almost) the same moment as the app's photo, for the
comparison shot (issue #55).

Privacy: it is never saved. It isn't a ``CapturedPhoto``, so it can't be handed to
``PhotoSaver``; it is never written to a file, added to Photos, signed, shared or logged.
``ComparisonModel`` turns it into a picture and a metadata list for the compare view and then
drops it; the picture and list are dropped when the view closes.

## `PhoneUpdate`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/WatchMessages.swift`

```swift
struct PhoneUpdate
```

What the phone sends the watch: the camera's status, and the result of the watch's last photo
request when there is one.

Every reply to a ``WatchCommand`` is one, and the phone also sends one whenever the status
changes or a photo ends while the watch app is reachable.

## `PhoneUpdate/init(status:result:unsignedReason:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/WatchMessages.swift`

```swift
init(status: WatchStatus, result: WatchResult? = nil, unsignedReason: SignatureStatus.UnsignedReason? = nil)
```

Creates an update.

- Parameters:
  - status: The camera's status.
  - result: A new result, or `nil`.
  - unsignedReason: Why the result's photos aren't all signed, or `nil` when they are or
    there is no result.

## `PhoneUpdate/result`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/WatchMessages.swift`

```swift
let result: WatchResult?
```

The result of the last photo the watch asked for, or `nil` when there is no new result.

## `PhoneUpdate/status`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/WatchMessages.swift`

```swift
let status: WatchStatus
```

The camera's status.

## `PhoneUpdate/unsignedReason`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/WatchMessages.swift`

```swift
let unsignedReason: SignatureStatus.UnsignedReason?
```

Why the result's photos aren't all signed, when ``result`` is
``WatchResult/saved(isSigned:)`` with `false` or ``WatchResult/savedSeveral(allSigned:)``
with `false`; otherwise `nil`. It is also `nil` from a phone app that doesn't send it.

Signing is the person's choice and off by default (issue #80), so an interface shows
``SignatureStatus/UnsignedReason/turnedOff`` as plain status, and any other reason as a
photo that couldn't be signed.

Optional, so the message stays readable both ways between apps of this protocol version
with and without it (``WatchMessageCoding/version`` is unchanged).

  - nopeImage: the app's picture, or `nil`.
  - phoneImage: The phone's picture, or `nil`.
  - nopeFile: What the app's shown file is, or `nil`.
  - phoneFormat: The phone's format.
  - phoneDimensions: The phone's size, in pixels.
  - metadata: The metadata side by side.
  - secondPhotoDelay: The time between the starts of the two photos.
  - mirrorsPhoneImage: Whether to mirror the phone's picture to match the app's.

## `PhotoDetails`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/PhotoDetails.swift`

```swift
struct PhotoDetails
```

"What's in this photo": the facts about one photo the app just saved, read back from the
saved files (issue #53).

``PhotoSaver`` builds it from each file's read-back (the project's own tag reader and
ImageIO), the metadata plan, the signer's manifest summary and the measured timings, and keeps
it in ``SavedPhoto/details``. Every value is what the file holds or what was measured; a
value the file doesn't hold is `nil` or empty, never estimated.

Privacy: built in memory from the just-saved photo and shown on screen only; nothing is
logged, stored or copied. The app can't reopen older photos (add-only access), so details
exist only for photos taken in this session.

``MetadataComparison`` builds its items, so a repeated tag is listed once and a
repeated block once with its count.

## `PhotoDetails/build(files:plan:look:signature:timings:rawClipping:distortionCorrection:options:pairing:capture:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/PhotoDetails.swift`

```swift
static func build(files: [CheckedFile], plan: MetadataPlan, look: DevelopedLook, signature: SignatureStatus, timings: SaveTimings, rawClipping: RAWClippingResult? = nil, distortionCorrection: DistortionCorrection? = nil, options: DevelopedFileOptions = DevelopedFileOptions(), pairing: RAWPairing = .heif, capture: CaptureReport? = nil) -> PhotoDetails
```

Builds the details of one saved photo.

- Parameters:
  - files: Each saved file with its read-back, in the order they were built.
  - plan: The metadata plan the files were written with.
  - look: The look HEIF and JPEG were developed with.
  - signature: The photo's signature status.
  - timings: The measured timings.
  - rawClipping: The clipping counted at capture time, or `nil`.
  - distortionCorrection: The lens's distortion correction for a processed fallback, or
    `nil`.
  - options: The options the HEIF and JPEG copies were developed with.
  - pairing: Which copy the person pairs with the RAW in Photos.
  - capture: What the camera reported, or `nil`.
- Returns: The details.

```swift
let capture: CaptureReport?
```

What the camera reported for this photo (shutter, ISO and lens), kept in memory with the
details for the session, or `nil` for details made outside ``PhotoSaver``.

(EXIF 3.0, 4.6.5), in the time zone of `OffsetTimeOriginal` or `OffsetTime` ("+01:00"),
or the device's time zone when the file has no offset; `nil` for a blank or malformed date.

- Parameter inventories: The read-backs.
- Returns: The date.

## `PhotoLibraryAsset/alternate`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/PhotoFile.swift`


The processed file paired with a RAW, or `nil`.

## `PhotoLibraryAsset/assets(for:pairing:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/PhotoFile.swift`

```swift
static func assets(for files: [PhotoFile], pairing: RAWPairing = .heif) -> [PhotoLibraryAsset]
```

Groups a photo's files into Photos assets.

A RAW and one processed file (HEIF if there is one, otherwise JPEG) become one asset with
the DNG as its main resource and the processed file as its alternate, as Apple documents
for RAW plus processed captures ("Capturing Photos in RAW and Apple ProRAW Formats", "Save
the photos to the photos library":
https://developer.apple.com/documentation/avfoundation/capturing-photos-in-raw-and-apple-proraw-formats).
Any other file is its own asset. When both a HEIF and a JPEG are saved, `pairing` says
which one is paired with the RAW (issue #50); the other is its own asset.

- Parameters:
  - files: The photo's files.
  - pairing: The processed copy to pair with the RAW when there are two. Defaults to
    ``RAWPairing/heif``.
- Returns: The assets to create, in one change.

## `PhotoLocation`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/PhotoLocation.swift`

```swift
struct PhotoLocation
```

Where a photo was taken: the only location values the app ever writes (issue #43).

Privacy rule (`docs/PRIVACY.md`, "Location"): a photo carries a location only when the person
turned the Location switch on and allowed location access. Then it carries exactly the
latitude, the longitude and, when the system knows it, the altitude, each with its EXIF
reference tag. No speed, direction, accuracy, satellite data, GPS time or date is written.
The values are the system's own, not rounded or changed.

## `PhotoLocation/altitude`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/PhotoLocation.swift`

```swift
let altitude: Double?
```

The altitude in meters above (positive) or below (negative) sea level, or `nil` when the
system has no valid altitude.

## `PhotoLocation/altitudeKey`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/PhotoLocation.swift`

```swift

```

`kCGImagePropertyGPSAltitude`.

## `PhotoLocation/altitudeRefKey`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/PhotoLocation.swift`


## `PhotoLocation/gpsProperties`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/PhotoLocation.swift`

```swift
var gpsProperties: [String : Any] { get }
```

The ImageIO GPS dictionary (`kCGImagePropertyGPSDictionary`) for the location.

EXIF 3.0, 4.6.6 ("GPS Attribute Information"): latitude and longitude are written as
positive values with a reference of `N`/`S` and `E`/`W` (`GPSLatitudeRef`,
`GPSLongitudeRef`), and altitude as a positive value with a reference of 0 (above sea
level) or 1 (below) (`GPSAltitudeRef`). The keys are the values of ImageIO's
`kCGImagePropertyGPS…` constants (`CGImageProperties.h`), checked against the constants
by `NopeDevelopTests`.

## `PhotoPreview`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/PhotoPreview.swift`

```swift
struct PhotoPreview
```

A screen-size picture of a saved photo, kept compressed in memory for the Last Photo viewer
(docs/design/BUILD-SPEC.md, 3.14 and section 6, item 15).

It holds only pixels, encoded as a JPEG with the system encoder: nothing from the photo's
metadata (no location, date or device) and no Content Credentials; only what the encoder
writes to describe the picture itself (its size and color space). A viewer decodes it with
``SessionPhotos/picture(of:maximumPixelSize:)`` only for the page shown and its neighbors.

Privacy: it lives in memory only, never on disk, in a log or in a copy, and ``SessionPhotos``
releases it in the background and under memory pressure (AGENTS.md, "Photos never linger";
docs/PRIVACY.md, "Last photo and its details").

## `PhotoSaver`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/PhotoSaver.swift`

```swift
struct PhotoSaver
```

Turns a captured photo into saved files: develops the selected formats, signs them, reads
each one back against its allowlist, and adds only clean files to Photos.

The order is fixed, and each step is a protocol so the whole pipeline is tested with fakes:

1. Build the files. A Bayer RAW photo's DNG already has its metadata built from the
   allowlist by the capture backend; HEIF and JPEG are developed from it
   (``PhotoDeveloping``). A lens without RAW gives its processed fallback file.
2. Read each file back (``MetadataReading``) and check it with ``MetadataVerifier``.
   Any violation rejects the whole photo: nothing is saved (AGENTS.md, "Fail closed").
3. Sign each file (``PhotoSigning``), when signing is on and a signer exists, and check the
   signed file the same way. A signed file that fails is never saved: the unsigned file,
   which passed, is saved instead, and the status says it isn't signed
   (``SignatureStatus/UnsignedReason/signedFileFailedCheck``).
4. Add the files to Photos with add-only access (``PhotoLibrary``), a RAW and its processed
   file as one asset (``PhotoLibraryAsset/assets(for:pairing:)``, with the person's
   ``SaveSettings/rawPairing``).
5. Make the last-photo thumbnail (``PhotoThumbnailing``), the compressed screen-size preview
   for ``SessionPhotos`` (``PhotoPreviewing``), and the "What's in this photo" details (``PhotoDetails``) from the
   saved files, in memory.


    (``SessionPhotos``), or `nil` for none.
  - now: Reads the clock for the timings. Tests pass a scripted one.

## `PhotoSaver/save(_:settings:captureDuration:distortionCorrection:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/PhotoSaver.swift`

```swift
func save(_ photo: CapturedPhoto, settings: SaveSettings, captureDuration: Duration? = nil, distortionCorrection: DistortionCorrection? = nil) async throws(CaptureError) -> SavedPhoto
```

Saves one photo.

Side effects: may show the add-only Photos prompt; adds the photo to Photos.

- Parameters:
  - photo: The captured photo, with its metadata already built for the plan.
  - settings: The formats and metadata choices.
  - captureDuration: How long the camera took to return the photo, for the details, or
    `nil` when unknown.
  - distortionCorrection: For a processed fallback, the lens's distortion correction as
    the camera reported it (``Lens/distortionCorrection``), for the details; ignored for
    a RAW photo.
- Returns: What was saved, with its thumbnail and details.
- Throws: ``CaptureError/developmentFailed`` if a format can't be developed,
  ``CaptureError/metadataNotAllowed`` if any unsigned file fails the read-back (nothing is
  saved),
  or the library's ``CaptureError/photosAccessDenied`` or ``CaptureError/saveFailed``.

## `PhotoSaver/saveReturningFiles(_:settings:captureDuration:distortionCorrection:makesPreview:)`

internal, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/PhotoSaver.swift`

```swift
func saveReturningFiles(_ photo: CapturedPhoto, settings: SaveSettings, captureDuration: Duration?, distortionCorrection: DistortionCorrection? = nil, makesPreview: Bool = true) async throws(CaptureError) -> (saved: SavedPhoto, files: [PhotoFile], preview: PhotoPreview?)
```

Saves one photo, as ``save(_:settings:captureDuration:distortionCorrection:)`` does, and also returns the
files exactly as they were added to Photos, for the compare view of the comparison shot
(issue #55). The files stay in memory; the caller drops them.

Side effects: may show the add-only Photos prompt; adds the photo to Photos.

- Parameters:
  - photo: The captured photo, with its metadata already built for the plan.
  - settings: The formats and metadata choices.
  - captureDuration: How long the camera took to return the photo, or `nil`.
  - distortionCorrection: For a processed fallback, the lens's distortion correction, or
    `nil`.
  - makesPreview: Whether to make the preview; `false` saves the work when the caller
    keeps none.
- Returns: What was saved, the saved files in the order they were built, and the
  compressed preview for ``SessionPhotos`` (`nil` when not asked for, without a
  previewer, or when the file can't be decoded).
- Throws: The same errors as ``save(_:settings:captureDuration:distortionCorrection:)``.

## `PhotoSigning`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/PhotoFile.swift`

```swift
protocol PhotoSigning : Sendable
```

The hook where Content Credentials signing plugs in: after the files are built and before
they are read back and saved.

`NopeSigning` implements it with a fresh Secure Enclave key for every photo (AGENTS.md,
"Every photo is signed, with a fresh key per photo"). ``PhotoSaver`` calls it for each file
when ``MetadataSettings/signsPhotos`` is on. The signed file is then read back like any
other: its manifest may add only the block the allowlist names for a signed file
(``MetadataPlan/allowlist(for:signed:)``), and a DNG's RAW image data must not change.

With no signer, files are saved unsigned and the status says so
(``SignatureStatus/unsigned(_:)`` with ``SignatureStatus/UnsignedReason/unavailable``).

## `PhotoSigning/sign(_:)`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/PhotoFile.swift`

```swift
func sign(_ file: PhotoFile) async throws -> PhotoFile
```

Signs one file.

- Parameter file: The file to sign.
- Returns: The same file with a Content Credentials manifest.
- Throws: Any error; the file is then saved unsigned, and the status says signing failed.

## `PhotoSizeEstimate`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/DeviceStatus.swift`

```swift
enum PhotoSizeEstimate
```

The largest a saved photo's files can be, estimated before the photo is taken.

The estimates are upper bounds chosen to warn early; they are assumptions until measured on a
device (docs/TESTING.md, "Low storage"):
- Bayer RAW: at most 16 bits a photosite (DNG 1.7, "BitsPerSample"), 2 bytes a pixel, whether
  or not it is compressed.
- HEIF: 1 byte a pixel, several times what the system HEVC encoder writes at full quality.
- JPEG at full quality: 2.5 bytes a pixel.
- Each file adds up to 1 MB for its metadata, preview and Content Credentials manifest.

## `PhotoThumbnail`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/PhotoThumbnail.swift`

```swift
struct PhotoThumbnail
```

A small picture of a photo the app just saved, for the last-photo thumbnail beside the shutter
(issue #36).

The app has add-only Photos access and can't read the library, so the thumbnail is made from
the saved file while it is still in memory (``PhotoThumbnailing``).

Privacy: the pixels live in memory only, for this session. They are never written to disk,
logged or copied (AGENTS.md, "Photos never linger").

## `RAWClipping`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/RAWClipping.swift`

```swift
struct RAWClipping
```

How much of a RAW photo is clipped: the photosites at or near the white level (highlights
that the sensor recorded as full) and at or near the black level (shadows with no signal
above the sensor's zero), per color of the color filter (issue #53).

The capture adapter counts every photosite of the RAW's active area in the photo's own
sensor buffer (`AVCapturePhoto.pixelBuffer`) at capture time, against the black and white
levels of the camera's DNG. Nothing is estimated: a photo whose buffer can't be read that way
gets a ``RAWClippingResult/notMeasured(_:)`` with the reason instead.

Definition: a highlight photosite is one at or above the white level less ``margin`` of the
range between the black and white levels; a shadow photosite is one at or below the black
level plus the same margin. The sensor may saturate a little below the white level the DNG
records, so this counts what the file says is full, not what the sensor did.

Privacy: counts only, kept in memory with the photo's details; never logged or stored.

## `RAWClipping/margin`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/RAWClipping.swift`

```swift
static let margin: Double
```

How near the white or black level a photosite counts as clipped: 0.5% of the range
between the two levels. A choice, not a platform value: near enough to the levels that
the photosite holds no usable detail, wide enough to include values a step or two off
the level.

## `RAWPairing`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/RAWPairing.swift`

```swift
enum RAWPairing
```

Which processed copy Photos keeps together with the RAW when RAW, HEIF and JPEG are all
saved (issue #50).

A Photos asset holds one main resource and one alternate (`PHAssetResourceType.photo` and
`.alternatePhoto`), so a RAW can be paired with only one processed copy; the other one is
saved as its own asset. The choice changes nothing about how each file is developed, what
metadata it carries, or how it is signed. ``SaveSettings/rawPairing`` holds it.


```swift
case heif
```

The HEIF is paired with the RAW and the JPEG is its own photo. The default.

## `RAWPairing/jpeg`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/RAWPairing.swift`

```swift
case jpeg
```

The JPEG is paired with the RAW and the HEIF is its own photo, for sharing to places
that accept JPEG only.

## `SeriesScreenKeeper`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/SeriesScreenKeeper.swift`

```swift
@MainActor final class SeriesScreenKeeper
```

Keeps the screen awake and dimmed while an interval series runs, and puts both back when it
ends (issue #48).

During a series the screen mustn't lock, or the camera stops and the series with it; dimming
saves battery over a long series (AGENTS.md, "Battery"). This is the only place the app turns
the idle timer off, and it is turned on again whenever the series ends or is stopped, the
camera screen goes away, or the app leaves the foreground.

An interface calls ``update(isSeriesRunning:isInForeground:)`` whenever either value changes,
shows a "Brighten Screen" control over the whole screen while ``isDimmed``, and announces the
change to VoiceOver. ``brighten()`` restores the brightness while the series continues.

## `SeriesScreenKeeper/brighten()`

public, declared in `Sources/NopeCore/SeriesScreenKeeper.swift`

```swift
@MainActor func brighten()
```

Restores the brightness while the series continues; the screen still doesn't lock.

```swift
@MainActor static let dimmedBrightness: Double
```

The brightness during a series, from 0 to 1. A screen already darker isn't changed. An
assumption to be checked on a device: dark enough to save power, bright enough to see the
progress.

```swift
struct SessionPhoto
```
